U2 Wind Back the Years With Classics, Dolly Parton Tribute for Thanksgiving Eve NFL Halftime Show

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Rock Hall-inducted group headlined the halftime show, which streamed live late Wednesday, Nov. 25 on Netflix.

The legendary Irish rock band, as they’ve done so often in the past, stepped into the cauldron, rocked out, and gave everyone what they were looking for.

On this occasion, Bono, The Edge, Adam Clayton and Larry Mullen Jr. stepped into the colosseum that is the NFL‘s first-ever Thanksgiving Eve game at SoFi Stadium, pitting the Los Angeles Rams against the visiting Green Bay Packers. Explore Bono U2 See latest videos, charts and news

The Rock Hall-inducted group headlined the halftime show, which streamed live late Wednesday, Nov. 25 on Netflix. Related Dolly Parton to Receive CNN Heroes Legacy Award, Miley Cyrus to Accept on Her Behalf ESSENCE Festival of Culture Moving On From New Orleans After Three Decades: 'Memories Were Made -- And So Was History' Noah Kahan Caps Epic Year With 'Live From the Great Divide' Album

At the very top, the four bandmates took the long walk to the centerfield as a choir recited the lyrics of “I Still Haven’t Found What I’m Looking For.”

“Thank you for having us,” Bono remarked over Mullen’s iconic, military-style drum pattern that launched the mini-set proper with “Sunday Bloody Sunday,” one of the band’s early political anthems. The production stepped up several gears as a marching band assembled and the song shapeshifted, interpolating The Supremes’ “Stop! In The Name of Love.”

Then, a change in mood as U2 paid tribute to Dolly Parton, the late queen of country’s smiling image splashed on three sails towering over the circular, center stage. Bono dueted with Dolly’s vocals for “Torn,” her very last recording which appears on the band’s 16th and latest studio album, Carnaval de Luz.

And, in a moment that earned a roar from the audience, the band slotted seamlessly into another Joshua Tree number, “Where The Streets Have No Name.” The sails came down, and U2 got down to rockstar business — with some support from scores of backing singers.

Without missing a beat, Bono and Co. reached into their glorybox with a rendition of “Pride (In The Name of Love)” a tribute to the assassinated civil rights leader Dr. Martin Luther King Jr., which appeared on their 1984 LP The Unforgettable Fire. And with that, U2 was done and it was game on.

Carnaval de Luz this month debuted at No. 1 on three Billboard charts, including No. 1 on Top Rock Albums, Vinyl Albums and Indie Store Album Sales, and became the band’s 14th top 10 on the Billboard 200 (opening at No. 4). It’s their 12th No. 1 on the Official U.K. Albums Chart and Australia’s ARIA Chart.

U2 this year chalks up its 50th anniversary, the celebrations for which will accelerate next year when the band returns to stadiums around the globe with Carnaval De Luz presents: The Greatest Show on Earth, its first stadium tour since 2019. Watch highlights from the Thanksgiving Eve halftime show and check out the setlist below. Stream the full performance here on Netflix.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Is withdrawal rate modeling too simplistic to be useful to most people?

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

My financial planning and my asset allocation is based on my withdrawal rate. My entire financial life is based on withdrawal rates.

I have a baseline withdrawal rate that I feel comfortable with. I select my asset allocation so that if there is a 50% market drop, my withdrawal rate stays within a certain bound. If I want to up my spending or make a large spend (gifting, DAF, setting up a trust, second home, house remodel, etc) I look at what that does to my withdrawal rate, and the corresponding effect on my asset allocation.

It is a stochastic approach. It is not perfect, but I feel comfortable with it.

An alternative is a specific budget with a LMP. I would not feel comfortable with that. It sounds deterministic, but it is not.

Of course, you can also do a combination of the two.

Fosun International Named "Sustainability Leading Enterprise" by Bloomberg Businessweek/Chinese Edition

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

HONG KONG, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- On 25 November, the 8th "ESG Leading Enterprises 2026" award ceremony, co-organized by Bloomberg Businessweek/Chinese Edition and Deloitte, a globally renowned accounting firm, was successfully held in Hong Kong. The event was honored to feature a keynote speech and award presentation by Mr. Joseph H.L. Chan, JP, Under Secretary for Financial Services and the Treasury of the Government of Hong Kong Special Administrative Region. Fosun International was honored as a "Sustainability Leading Enterprise", in recognition of its outstanding achievements and strong market position across ESG strategy, corporate governance, social responsibility, environmental protection, information disclosure, and stakeholder engagement.

This year, 9 companies were honored as "Sustainability Leading Enterprises" in Category I - market capitalization over HKD12 billion, all of which are major Hong Kong listed companies, including Henderson Land Development, Sun Hung Kai Properties, HUTCHMED, The Hong Kong and China Gas, China Overseas Land & Investment, China Unicom, etc.

Operating Responsibly Worldwide, Earning Sustained ESG Recognition

Rooted in China, Fosun has been developing globally while remaining committed to its two key growth drivers of "globalization" and "innovation". Fosun is one of the few Chinese companies that is equipped with global operational and investment capabilities, and has built strong capabilities in technology and innovation. Drawing on its well-established global business network, Fosun operates responsibly in more than 40 countries and regions, helping improve lives and create sustainable value worldwide.

In recent years, Fosun has demonstrated outstanding performance in various aspects of ESG. To date, Fosun International's MSCI ESG Rating has been upgraded to the highest rating of AAA. It has been included in the S&P Global Sustainability Yearbook 2026 and also named among the Top 1% in the S&P Global Sustainability Yearbook (China Edition) 2026 for the third consecutive year. Its Hang Seng Sustainability Rating has been upgraded from AA- to AA+. It has also been selected as a constituent of the Hang Seng Corporate Sustainability Benchmark Index for the seventh consecutive year and included as a constituent of the Hang Seng ESG 50 Index. Additionally, Fosun International's FTSE Russell ESG score has consistently been higher than the global industry average and it has remained a constituent of the FTSE4Good Index Series for five consecutive years.

Leveraging Innovation to Deliver Lasting Social Impact

Fosun adheres to an innovation-driven strategy, continuously delivering innovative achievements across its businesses to better serve society. As of 30 June 2026, Fosun Pharma had cumulatively supplied more than 460 million vials of artesunate for injection worldwide, saving more than 92 million patients with severe malaria. Its independently developed HANSIZHUANG (serplulimab) is the world's first anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody approved for the first-line treatment of small cell lung cancer, and the first anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody approved for perioperative treatment of gastric cancer. It is now available in more than 50 countries and regions. HLX43, a core asset in the innovative pipeline, is a potential best-in-class (BIC) broad-spectrum anti-tumor PD-L1 antibody-drug conjugate (ADC). It has demonstrated preliminary clinical efficacy characterized by high efficacy and low toxicity across multiple solid tumors, including non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), with over 1,500 patients enrolled globally.

Fosun remains committed to global cooperation and shared success, striving to make medical technologies more accessible and affordable to more patients. Yi Kai Da (ejilunsai injection), China's first approved CAR-T cell therapy, is now available across a network of over 190 treatment centers spanning 29 provinces, autonomous regions, and municipalities nationwide. More than 1,500 patients with relapsed or refractory lymphoma have received the therapy, with the initial clinical trial patient remaining cancer-free for more than eight years. This year, the da Vinci SP surgical system was included in the catalog under the "Hong Kong and Macau Medicine and Equipment Connect" scheme. It was also admitted into the Special Review Procedure for Innovative Medical Devices by the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA). The Ion Robotic Bronchoscopy (Ion System) received marketing approval and was launched in the Chinese mainland, extending the use of minimally invasive robotic technology beyond surgery to the diagnosis and treatment of lung diseases. Exablate Neuro is a Magnetic Resonance-guided Focused Ultrasound (MRgFUS) system that has treated over 30,000 patients across more than 210 medical centers worldwide, offering a new treatment option for patients with medication-refractory essential tremor or tremor-dominant Parkinson's disease.

Actively Responding to Climate Change and Promoting Low-Carbon Transition

To address climate change, Fosun actively supports China's "dual carbon" goals of carbon peaking and carbon neutrality, advancing initiatives in carbon neutrality, energy conservation, emissions reduction, and climate risk management. In 2026, the Company continued its voluntary disclosure practice by releasing its fourth Climate Information Disclosures Report, further enhancing the systematic approach and transparency of its climateârelated disclosures. The Bund Finance Center (BFC), the Group's main office and a landmark in Shanghai, introduced and followed the LEED Certification, which is hailed as the "Oscar Award" in the green building industry, from the early planning stage. BFC also obtained the LEED Platinum certification in 2022 with the highest score globally.

Preserving and Sharing Intangible Cultural Heritage, Promoting Sustainable Oriental Lifestyle Aesthetics

Over the years, Fosun has taken a comprehensive approach to preserving intangible cultural heritage, integrating it into modern daily life while weaving sustainability into oriental lifestyle aesthetics. Fosun's Grand Yuyuan is working to create an urban cultural parlor that showcases intangible cultural heritage. Take the Yuyuan Lantern Festival, a national intangible cultural heritage event, as an example, in 2026, the Year of the Horse, the festival featured 6 themed lantern zones for the first time and drew a total of 6 million visitors. Supported by Fosun's global platform, the Yuyuan Lantern Festival continues to put its commitment to carbon neutrality into practice, leveraging low-carbon technologies to create environmentally friendly lantern displays. It has also expanded internationally, with editions held in Paris, France, Hanoi, Vietnam, and Bangkok, Thailand. The festival has become a vital platform for Sino-foreign cultural exchange, continuously telling "China Stories" to the world and conveying the vision of sustainable living.

Committed to Business for Good, Actively Giving Back to Society

In order to better promote the fulfillment and implementation of corporate social responsibility, Fosun Foundation was established in 2012. It has been making unremitting efforts in areas such as global emergency relief, rural revitalization, health, education, culture and art, youth development, to create social value. Of which, since its launch at the end of 2017, Fosun Foundation's Rural Doctors Program has covered 78 counties in 16 provinces, cities, and autonomous regions, supporting 25,000 rural doctors and benefiting 3 million rural families.

Looking ahead, Fosun will continue to pursue innovation and operate responsibly worldwide, striving to build a more responsible, inclusive, and sustainable future.

Nepal: Predatory Lending Abuses Exacerbated by Conflict in the Gulf

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Click to expand Image Loan shark victims demand justice after 38 days of protest in Kathmandu, Nepal, March 7, 2024. © 2024 Skanda Gautam/SOPA Images/Sipa via AP Photo Thousands of borrowers face exorbitant interest on fraudulent loan agreements taken out to fund labor migration and other needs.The Nepali government has criminalized abusive practices, but police and courts often side with loan sharks against distressed borrowers.Lost job opportunities due to war in the Gulf region have deepened the crisis facing many borrowers.

(Tokyo) – Authorities in Nepal should hold illegal moneylenders accountable for abuses against borrowers, including unlawful land dispossession, arbitrary eviction, threats and acts of violence, and sexual exploitation, Human Rights Watch said today. Conflicts in the Gulf region, where many Nepalis are migrant workers facing exorbitant recruitment fees, have made more people vulnerable to extortion by illegal lenders.

“Despite government commitments to protect borrowers, illegal practices are rife in Nepal’s informal credit sector, leaving those with the fewest resources dependent on moneylenders who can take everything they own,” said Meenakshi Ganguly, Asia deputy director at Human Rights Watch. “The government of Prime Minister Balendra Shah should carry out the government’s commitments to ensure that the law against fraudulent and abusive lending is consistently enforced.”

Human Rights Watch interviewed 18 borrowers, as well as local government officials, lawyers, and activists from six districts in the Terai region where informal money lending is rampant, reviewed government reports, and examined over 50 cases in Nawalparasi district.

Human Rights Watch found that many families are struggling with unmanageable debt particularly as migrant workers in the Gulf States during 2026 have faced reduced incomes or involuntary travel delays ahead of starting jobs due to the conflict between the US, Israel, and Iran. War-induced disruption amplified pre-existing abuses, including reliance on unauthorized individual agents and unreasonable recruitment fees. Human Rights Watch previously found that companies in Gulf states play a critical role in driving these illegal costs by refusing to cover costs and fees to recruiters that they know will be borne by workers.

Government investigations have shown that, under Nepal’s informal credit practices, a borrower signs or thumbprints an unsecured personal loan contract, known locally as kapali tamasuk, which can falsely list a principal amount three to ten times more than the cash received. These loans, known as meter byaj, in some cases compound at annual rates ranging from 36 percent to 60 percent or more, compared with less than 10 percent charged by banks.

As low-income households that had relied on remittances disrupted by the war have shifted their limited resources toward expenses for food, fuel, and healthcare, they have been increasingly unable to repay their loans, leading to a surge in defaults. In response, some moneylenders are forcing evictions or land transfers, including through violence, in the worst cases tying people into generational debt. Nepal authorities are not pushing for effective long term solutions beyond encouraging and facilitating debtors and creditors to enter pre-court settlements.

Following the outbreak of conflict in the Persian Gulf, in March 2026, the Nepal government suspended both institutional and individual labor permits to protect its citizens, including to Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, Qatar, and Kuwait, with an estimated 2,000 Nepalis denied labor permits daily. Thousands were stopped from travelling at the international airport despite possessing valid medical clearances, verified visas, and binding employment contracts, while administrative delays also led foreign firms to cancel labor contracts. However, the authorities failed to step in to protect aspiring migrant laborers who still had to service their high interest meter byaj loans, leaving them exposed to violent enforcement by loan sharks.

“They did not just hit my father to get the money,” said one victim. “They beat him in the middle of the road during the day so that everyone else in the village would see what happens when you cannot pay the meter byaj. The local goons told us, ‘If you don’t sign over the land title today, your family will receive a corpse tomorrow.’”

While abuses, including violent attacks and forced evictions, appear to be increasing due to conflict related disruptions, many moneylenders have long wielded political and economic power over borrowers. Claiming that the justice system routinely favors the moneylenders, borrowers have staged three extended protests in Kathmandu over the last four years—the longest for 442 days—and three long marches. Each time, the government agreed to resolve debt issues but failed to honor its commitments to protect victims. Consequently, the borrowers have regularly protested at district and provincial levels as well.

Although government has criminalized such loans, borrowers and activists in Madhesh Province said that police have repeatedly refused to file reports when victims seek protection. Instead, they said, the police typically dismiss abuses and intimidation by moneylenders as “private civil disputes.”

“When the moneylender came to break our door, we ran to the police, but the officers told us to go home and settle personal transactional disputes ourselves,” said a Rautahat district migrant worker.

The police also back moneylenders when they use signed blank checks and banking laws to threaten defaulters with arrest, or use informal deeds as the basis to seize land and evict families. “The lender made me sign a blank check when he gave me a loan, and later on he filled it for 10 lakhs (US$6,500) and sent the police to threaten me with jail when the check bounced,” said a Siraha district farmer.

Borrowers also cite cases in which the authorities do not intervene even when lenders use patently unlawful loan agreements to obtain asset foreclosure orders. “The lender told me openly that he owns the courts and the administration,” said Shiv Shanker from Gaur, Rautahat district, who after facing abuses from a moneylender, is leading protests. “When the state shuts its doors on a poor farmer, death feels like the only place left to hide from this meter byaj.”

On May 5, 2023, the government criminalized informal loans as an “improper transaction,” and declared that forging loan contracts, threatening borrowers, or fabricating loan amounts, are punishable by up to seven years in prison. However, enforcement remains weak. Successive governments have established inquiry commissions to address the crisis, but over 65,000 complaints registered with inquiry commissions remain unresolved due to a lack of legal provisions and administrative bottlenecks.

These failures have contributed to deadly outcomes including suicide. Santidevi Mahato from Biratnagar died by suicide on June 21, following threats from a moneylender because she could not service her debt, people interviewed said. After borrowing 150,000 Nepali rupees (US$975) in October 2015, she had repaid 400,000 Nepali rupees (US$2,600) in installments. Yet, violating their agreement, the moneylender seized her land and threatened to evict her from her house.

Under international human rights law and standards, governments have the obligation to take steps to protect people from serious rights abuses caused by private actors, such as moneylenders, including through investigating and prosecuting serious crimes.

On July 17, the government led by Prime Minister Balendra Shah concluded a nine-point pact with protest leaders to eliminate predatory lending, after debt victims began a march to Kathmandu. The government should take immediate legal and policy steps to institutionalize its commitment, Human Rights Watch said. Nepal’s donors and international partners should assist the government to establish systematic debt relief, and grievance mechanisms that can support debt restructuring.

“The Balendra Shah government was elected with the promise of change, to overturn existing oppressive structures,” Ganguly said. “More closely regulating moneylenders and ending their chokehold on farmers and laborers is a crucial step in ensuring lasting social justice.”

Loan Sharks and Migrant Workers from Nepal

Nepal’s informal credit system is rooted in its feudal history, where local elites used predatory debt to trap peasants in tenancy and systems of generational servitude such as Kamaiya, Haliya, and Haruwa-Charuwa.

The present-day meter byaj system evolved around a spurt in labor migration during the decade-long civil war in Nepal, from 1996 to 2006, and rapidly spread after the 2007 political unrest in the Terai region. An estimated 7 million Nepalis work abroad, about half in India and a majority of the rest in Gulf countries, contributing a third of the national GDP. For many households, migration became one of the few viable ways to earn a living, while the absence of accessible and affordable institutional credit left them dependent on informal lenders to finance the costs of securing employment abroad.

Some local moneylenders and corrupt agents demanded exorbitant fees to help rural youth find jobs abroad, particularly in the Gulf States and Malaysia. Land or property was borrowers’ main collateral, but they often later discovered that their contracts were fraudulent, including falsified sums and forged documents. The schemes exploited bureaucratic complicity at government land offices to transfer property titles despite contracts being illegal, and borrowers lost their assets due to a lack of protection from the government.

In interviews, debt victims described devastating cycles in which migrant workers are forever bound to serving a predatory moneylender, while the authorities fail to intervene. Ramnath Musahar from Susta, Nawalparasi district, said the family borrowed to send two of his brothers to work in the Gulf States in 2015, but despite servicing the loan, their debt had grown enormously:

My brothers sent back Rs. 1,200,000 (US$8,000) from their wages, and we sold our plots [of land] to pay an extra Rs. 1,800,000 (US$11,000). But the lender went to the district court with a fake contract and claimed we still owe him Rs. 5,000,000 (US$33,000). The court looked only at the paper and our thumbprints; it did not look at our tears. The police came and threw our belongings into the dirt. We went from being independent farmers to beggars overnight.

Conflict Between the US, Israel, and Iran Exacerbates Debt Risks

Since the US and Israel began their assault on Iran on February 28, 2026, and Iran subsequently blocked and attacked ships traveling through the Strait of Hormuz and attacked sites across the Gulf States, the fallout is being felt far beyond the region.

In Nepal, UN agencies warned that “rising fuel prices, closure of shipping routes, and growing financial uncertainty are placing mounting pressure on economies, labor markets and vulnerable households.” According to the World Bank, Nepal’s real GDP growth could fall from 3.67 percent in 2024 to 2.3 percent in 2026 due to reduced tourism, higher transport costs, and supply chain snags. Rising inflation, a weakening currency leading to a rise in import costs, a spike in fuel costs, and stalled fertilizer supply have placed additional pressure on low-income households, and increased demands for overseas employment.

Hoping to seek jobs to support their families, many rural youths have taken loans from loan sharks, who have responded by raising rates. Many families were stuck servicing those loans, even as people were unable to travel because of conflict-related disruptions.

Workers already employed in the Gulf States are facing job losses, unpaid leave, wage reductions, and other disruptions that have reduced their ability to keep sending remittances. Compared with the same period the previous year, Gulf-bound labor approvals plummeted by 52 percent—from 228,165 down to 109,249—between the outbreak of the war in late February and mid-September, according to Department of Foreign Employment data. At least two Nepali workers have been killed due to the conflict, and 15 injured.

Many are returning to Nepal stripped of their anticipated income, and unable to service their loans. The authorities have failed to act as moneylenders crack down on defaulters, beating them, evicting them, and forcing land or asset transfers. The lenders are also seeking court-ordered asset foreclosures and even arrest warrants based on the loan contracts, including contracts that borrowers alleged contained fabricated loan amounts. As a Mahotari district borrower observed, “The law says meter byaj is a crime, but police demanded receipts I never got, so the thief walks free, and I am made the criminal.”

Women-led households, Dalits (considered an “untouchable” caste), and Indigenous ethnic communities have even fewer protections from violence such as beatings and sexual violence due to their caste, gender, and discrimination. One woman from Dhanusha district said she was raped by a moneylender under threat of eviction.

My husband was trapped in a labor camp abroad and couldn’t send money. The lender came to my house with the contract and said he would evict my children the next morning unless I “compromised” with him. He held our roof over my head like a weapon. I had to endure his abuse in silence because a poor woman in this village has no voice against a powerful man who holds her land paper.

Abuses by Moneylenders

While the conflict has exacerbated the crisis, debt-ridden families had already proliferated in Nepal, particularly in Terai districts. The authorities have failed to protect these families.

Chirai Chamar, a widowed Dalit worker in Paklihawa, Nawalparasi district, while struggling to gain control of her inherited family land, had borrowed 20,000 Nepali rupees (US$130). A moneylender offered to help with the legalities in exchange for a portion of that land. “A moneylender, from whom I had borrowed Rs. 20,000, came to me saying he will help me to acquire all the papers required to own my family land, and in return I need to give him two katthas (650 square meters) of land,” she said.

He then grabbed an additional four katthas (1336 square meters), paying Chamar a grossly undervalued sum of Rs. 150,000 (US$970). When rights activists intervened to reclaim Chamar’s land and restore her livelihood, local thugs threatened them and destroyed Chamar’s crops. Despite complaints, the police and the local officials failed to assist her.

Ashamaya (name changed), a resident of Thecho, Lalitpur, supported her family by running a small tea shop. However, when her father-in-law fell ill, she had to close the business to care for him. Left without income as the medical expenses mounted, she borrowed money from a moneylender, who charged an extortionate annual interest rate of 120 percent, which she could not pay. Under pressure, she took on another usurious loan simply to pay off the old one.

Although she settled that debt by selling a piece of land, she had to borrow again, this time to cover her husband’s medical costs, but she no longer had enough land for collateral. The moneylender coerced her into sex with multiple men after he forced her to sign a new contact for Rs. 2500,000 (UDS$16,167), amounting to five times her initial debt.

Seeking a Remedy

Debt victims have started to organize under various banners and groups, demanding that the government protect them from abusive loan sharks. In 2022, after hundreds walked barefoot for weeks from districts including Siraha, Dhanusha, Mahottari, Sarlahi, Kapilvastu, and Nawalparasi to stage protests in Kathmandu, the government at that time establisheda three-member Commission for the Investigation of Loan Sharking (CILS) in April 2023, to collect complaints, study structural issues, and resolve loan-sharking disputes.

By May 21, 2023, when the District Administration Offices stopped accepting these complaints, over 28,000 had been filed across 67 districts, with an alarming 21,552 cases concentrated in Madhesh Province. However, the commission only resolved and closed 5,115 loan disputes. Several victims reported abuses by district offices. “We went to the Chief District Officer expecting justice, but instead, the police and the lender ganged up on us, warned us that going to court would mean total land auction, and scared us into signing an agreement to pay half the inflated sum,” said a woman from Siraha.

As complaints persisted, including of violent encounters in which the police failed to intervene, the authorities largely resorted to symbolic agreements with the victims, followed by the repeated formation of new commissions. A commission was set up in March 2024, led by former high court judge Tej Bahadur Karki, and another in March 2025, led by Baburam Regmi. In November 2025, the Regmi Commission reported a total of 41,409 complaints registered in 43 districts. Of these, 6,393 cases had been resolved, while 35,016 complaints remain pending.

Each commission exposed the vast scale of the crisis but political commitments to break the stranglehold of moneylenders remain unfulfilled. Nawalparasi’s Deputy Chief District Officer Poshan Raj Bhandari told Human Rights Watch that, “The existing law will not be enough to resolve this problem; the government needs to think of a separate legal process to resolve this.”

When Prime Minister Balendra Shah’s government was elected in March 2026 with a large majority, victims had cause for hope. However, instead of the government ensuring a collective debt moratorium, victims say that moneylenders have convinced the police to issue a surge of arrest warrants against debtors.

In response, victims have organized new protests. Nigra Nabin, a leader of the protest movement, said that based on “past experiences” the victims “had three long-standing demands” including the nullification of all illegal agreements and the underlying loan contracts that support them; a new law to institutionalize these actions and establish special legal procedures; and a separate entity dedicated to ensuring justice for the victims.

On July 9, the anti-loan shark movement began an 11-day justice march from Janakpur to Kathmandu. On the sixth day of the march, Home Affairs Minister Sudhan Gurung traveled to Nijgadh to hold direct talks with the protesters. Following three days of negotiations, the government accepted the three core demands and signed an updated nine-point deal, leading the protesters to call off the march.

The victims are waiting. One of them, Sobita Devi Mahato, said: “If this is not resolved, the only resort I have is to go to the Home Ministry and hang myself there.”

RecommendationsHalt arbitrary arrests of borrowers.Prosecute exploitative lenders for crimes against defaulters including violent attacks, sexual exploitation, and threats.Impose penalties for predatory lending practices, including aggressive debt collection through forced eviction or land grabs.Stop district authorities and police from carrying out foreclosures and evictions based on illegal loan agreements.Draft a new law to end illegal loan agreements and usury-related abuses. Establish a legal hold on all land transfers and evictions tied to disputed informal loans.Provide assistance, including debt relief where possible, to migrant workers in crisis because of sudden loss of work due to circumstances beyond their control, including war.Devise and enforce laws and policies that are designed to protect borrowers, including interest rate caps, requirements that borrowers have full and clear information about loan terms, and proper assessment of borrowers’ ability to repay loans. Consult with victims and stakeholders while drafting new laws and policies to dismantle the abusive informal moneylending system.Expand access to affordable institutional credit for low-income households and strengthen oversight of labor recruitment so that workers are not forced to rely on abusive informal lenders to finance migration or other essential expenses.Expand access to decent work and livelihoods in Nepal and strengthen social security so that households are less likely to rely on debt-financed labor migration.Use bilateral labor instruments such as labor agreements with governments of destination countries to ensure that companies hiring Nepali migrant workers cover the costs and fees related to their recruitment as per the employer pays principle, conduct proper due diligence and effective monitoring of Nepali recruiters, and have reimbursement mechanisms in instances when workers have paid fees.

K-beauty stocks rally as Korean brands top Amazon bestseller lists globally ahead of Black Friday

📅 2026-11-26  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

South Korean beauty stocks jumped Thursday as the country's skincare brands dominated Amazon's bestseller rankings ahead of Black Friday.

Shares of K-beauty giant APR climbed 3.77%, while cosmetic contracts manufacturers Kolmar Korea and Cosmax gained 5.46% and 2.91%, respectively.

APR shares, in particular, have rallied sharply this week, up 28%, defying a broader market sell-off in Seoul markets, with the benchmark Kospi index down 2.65% during the same period.

A cosmetic sector index tracked by Meritz Securities had gained 12% this week until Wednesday, according to a Thursday note from the brokerage.

The rally reflects renewed optimism over earnings growth following strong performances by K-beauty brands across major e-commerce platforms in the U.S. and Europe with Black Friday sales already underway, as well as increasingly attractive valuations following months of share price declines, according to Meritz.

K-beauty products topped Amazon's skincare bestseller rankings across six major markets — the U.S., the U.K., Germany, France, Spain and Italy — as of Nov. 25, according to data compiled by Meritz Securities. Other Korean beauty brands, including Biodance, Illiyoon and Anua, also ranked among the top 10 in the U.S., the data showed.

As of Thursday morning, Korean beauty products occupied several of the top spots in Amazon's U.S. beauty and personal care category, the e-commerce website showed.

Meritz Securities expects APR shares to maintain their positive momentum through the year-end, with a more substantial rebound in early 2027.

The brokerage named APR and Cosmax as its top picks in the cosmetics sector. Korean beauty brands have established an "overwhelming presence" in the bestseller rankings of major e-commerce platforms during Black Friday sales, prompting the market to reassess the growth's potential, the brokerage said.

Here's a roadmap to the Aaron Rodgers developments, one of the craziest stories in years

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you were traveling on Wednesday or otherwise not plugged in for one of the most rollicking, unpredictable days in years if not ever, you’re in luck.

We’ve reconstructed the full timeline as to the events regarding now-former Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers.

It actually started on Monday, when coach Mike McCarthy was asked whether he has considered using one of the team’s other three quarterbacks when the offense is struggling. McCarthy, whose body language in hindsight suggests he was dreading the question, said without equivocation, “Aaron Rodgers is our quarterback.”

On Tuesday, Rodgers took his usual reps at practice. He conducted his usual midweek press conference. Unbeknownst to most of the media or the general public, the Steelers were changing their play-calling procedures to have quarterbacks coach Tom Arth, and not McCarthy, communicate the calls to Rodgers through the speaker in his helmet.

Then came Tuesday night. At 11:10 p.m. ET, the Steelers issued a statement — not from coach Mike McCarthy or G.M. Omar Khan or owner Art Rooney II but from team spokesman Burt Lauten — that Mason Rudolph will start on Friday against the Broncos and Rodgers will be inactive.

The news forced the Broncos to make a quick pivot in their preparations from Rodgers to Rudolph. As one source put it, the Broncos weren’t sure whether it was a good thing or a bad thing.

It was definitely a bad thing, all things considered, for the Steelers.

By Wednesday morning, reports emerged that it was a coach’s decision. At 8:00 a.m. ET, McCarthy informed the team of the decision.

Rodgers wasn’t there. As it turns out, he was excused.

Enter Pat McAfee. He teased a surprise guest at 1:40 p.m. ET. As expected, it was Rodgers.

Rodgers took a flamethrower to McCarthy, airing out dirty laundry that created a major mess for his now-former team. He exposed the fact that, at halftime of the Week 11 loss to the Eagles, McCarthy broke down the locker-room gathering by saying, “1-2-3 Packers.”

Rodgers also said McCarthy was falling asleep during some meetings and missing other meetings, expressing (in passive-aggressive fashion) concern for McCarthy’s health.

But Rodgers also said it was nothing new, and that McCarthy had done the same things in Green Bay, when they worked there together a decade ago.

Rodgers also made it clear he wouldn’t stay with the Steelers. After his session with McAfee and company, Dulac reported that the Steelers were expected to release Rodgers. By the official close of business, they’d waived him.

A question lingers as to whether the Steelers will try to deny Rodgers the balance of his $22 million salary. It won’t be easy; they’ll need to both void the guarantees and wipe out his eligibility for termination pay (in the form of his remaining salary) under the Collective Bargaining Agreement.

McCarthy met with reporters on Wednesday, after all hell had broken loose. He admitted to the “1-2-3 Packers” comment, calling it an “[e]mbarrassing moment.”

McCarthy also said that Rodgers was benched after issuing an “ultimatum” that was “clearly outside of his job description.”

ESPNFL thereafter reported that McCarthy “has the full support” of the organization, and that the team is “not planning to make any immediate changes.” The key word may be “immediate"; with few viable options only two days before a Black Friday game against the Broncos, changes could come later.

The overriding takeaway is that someone with the Steelers knew or should have known that McCarthy and Rodgers couldn’t co-exist. Because, obviously, they didn’t co-exist.

If, as Rodgers claims, McCarthy believed Rodgers got him fired in Green Bay, McCarthy shouldn’t have wanted a reunion with Rodgers. And if, as Rodgers claims, McCarthy was doing the same kinds of things in Green Bay that he did in Pittsburgh, Rodgers shouldn’t have wanted a reunion with McCarthy.

The reunion has now ended abruptly, with seven games left in the 2026 regular season. It remains to be seen whether Rodgers wants to play with another team, or whether another team wants him. Any team that signs him risks being on the receiving end of a future gripe session from Rodgers in which he lists all the things that team and/or the coach did wrong — without ever acknowledging his own role in the reality that it didn’t work out.

The good news, if there is any, is this: If Rodgers never plays again, his final NFL pass will have been a touchdown.

Which is a better outcome than if he’d retired after the 2025 season. His final pass that year was a pick-six in the playoffs.

S&P warns Nvidia credit rating could be cut over 'rising' management and governance risk

📅 2026-11-26  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Nvidia's credit rating could be cut by one notch over the next 90 days if the company continues to have "deficiencies" in its management and governance framework, S&P Global Ratings said in the wake of the Jensen Huang's ouster and reinstatement as Nvidia CEO.

"News regarding the CEO and questions regarding potential export control violations have exposed vulnerabilities in Nvidia's management and governance framework, especially in regard to its board structure, composition, and effectiveness," S&P said in a Wednesday note.

Nvidia currently holds an AA rating, the third-highest level.

S&P has placed the chipmaker on "CreditWatch with negative implications," though any potential downgrade would be limited to one notch, to AA-, unless investigations or disclosures lead the agency to conclude that Nvidia's reputation or future prospects have also been harmed.

A downgrade can pressure bond prices, push yields higher and raise borrowing costs. Nvidia priced $25 billion of investment-grade senior notes in June, with maturities from 2028 through 2056, shortly after S&P upgraded the company from AA- to AA.

Nvidia has about $32.5 billion of bonds in the U.S. high-grade corporate bond index, according to Bloomberg.

S&P's Wednesday note follows Moody's Ratings changing its outlook on Nvidia to stable from positive on Nov. 18, citing governance concerns, and leaving that outlook unchanged after Huang's return on Monday.

In its note, S&P cited the "long tenure" of certain board members as a reason their independence may be "compromised" and said Nvidia's management and governance risk was "rising."

The ratings agency added that it remained unaware of any remedial action taken by the board to address what could be "meaningful" management and governance deficiencies.

Chairman Harvey Jones and early investor Tench Coxe have served on Nvidia's board since 1993, while Brooke Seawell joined in 1997. Mark Stevens served from 1993 to 2006 and rejoined the board in 2008.

S&P also flagged questions around geopolitical risk management and internal controls arising from Nvidia's exposure to China.

"While Nvidia management has delivered significant returns for its stakeholders over decades, weak executive oversight and lack of succession planning have brought questions around the "key man" risk to the forefront," S&P's Andrew Chang and David Tsui said.

Chang and Tsui noted that Huang's resignation and subsequent reinstatement did not resolve questions about the board's oversight of executive conduct.

The agency also intends to meet with Nvidia's board and management to discuss plans to address those deficiencies, particularly around "board independence, effective management oversight and a credible leadership succession plan."

Huang returned less than two weeks after Jones said Nvidia's directors did not intend to reinstate him. The board later voted unanimously to bring Huang back, sending Nvidia shares up as much as 36% during Monday's session.

Nvidia shares are down about 12.5% since Monday's close.

8 Best Steamy Shows for Adults Only You Can Stream Now

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There are some TV shows that are immediate guilty pleasures because of the type of content they present. It’s not that we feel guilty because they’re bad, but because we blush when we tell our friends and family about them. And yes, they're all pretty steamy! There’s nothing wrong with them; they’re just not safe for work! We’re here to celebrate eight steamy shows you can stream right now! And fret not, you’re not going to be alone!

From a gay hockey show to a period drama where love and marriage always involve intimacy, these eight shows are sex-positive, romantically-tinged, and genuinely juicy! These shows are so steamy, you might need to hide under the covers or shield your eyes! But the brilliance of these eight shows is that they’re more than just steamy; they have excellent storylines, thrills, shocks, and brilliant characters. While there are certainly many other shows that can be added to this roster, this smattering gives you a range of options from various genres and eras.

If you love cheap thrills, 56 Days is for you! Developed by Karyn Usher and Lisa Zwerling, and based on Catherine Ryan Howard's novel, the eight-episode erotic psychological thriller centers around Ciara Wyse (Dove Cameron) and wealthy architect Oliver Kennedy (Avan Jogia), who dive into an intense romance after meeting at a supermarket. Exactly 56 days later, an unidentifiable, decomposing body is discovered in the bathtub of Oliver’s luxury Boston apartment. Moving back and forth across their timeline, the series chronicles detectives Lee Reardon (Karla Souza) and Karl Connolly (Dorian Missick) as they investigate the murder and untangle the dark secrets both lovers are hiding. A bingeworthy mystery, 56 Days leads with a whodunnit hook before delivering an intense, steamy, sex-centric relationship thriller.

The story opens on Day 56, leading to an unrelenting mystery where the central suspects are all but reliable sources. Both leads harbor hidden motives and trust issues, creating tension over who is manipulating whom as their whirlwind relationship escalates. Yet through their individual missions, their physical relationship remains the only authentic thing. It’s a fast-burning relationship that is rooted in an intense, lust-driven affair. The narrative embraces high-gloss melodrama, dark secrets, and explicit adult content, prioritizing Ciara and Oliver’s bedroom scenes and sensationalism not to be provocative but to display the spectrum of their relationship. It’s unhinged and messy, but he’s delicious to watch from an outside perspective. 56 Days is an addictive premise that keeps you hooked until the bitter end.

Since Season 1, Bridgerton has gotten fans all hot and bothered because period romance is super steamy! The Shondaland drama based on Julia Quinn's book series follows the eight close-knit siblings of the powerful Bridgerton family as they navigate high society's competitive marriage market in search of true love. The Regency-era alternate history spotlights a different sibling's romantic journey amidst lavish balls, family duty, and social expectations. Along the way, the elite members of the "ton" are scrutinized by the anonymous gossip columnist Lady Whistledown (Julie Andrews), whose scandal sheets expose their deepest secrets and fuel society-wide intrigue. A beautifully shot series that blends extraordinary costumes, immaculate set pieces, and a modern pop soundtrack covered by a classical string quartet, Bridgerton is truly all about the intimacy. And the show never falls short of that fantasy.

Bridgerton emerged as a remarkably beloved series because it pairs explicit, emotionally charged intimacy with high-stakes era Romance told through modern pacing. Created by Chris Van Dusen, Bridgerton is heavy on the romance, scandal, and family dynamics, but when the season's lovers find their moments together, that's when Bridgerton truly heats up. Intimate scenes drive character arcs rather than serving as mere shock value. Whether it be sexual awakenings, intimate vulnerability, or shifting power dynamics built within the boudoir, these moments are genuine narrative moments that are earned. Each season builds longing through classic romance setups, from fake dating to enemies-to-lovers narratives, that make the payoff feel earned. With strict Regency social rules in mind, Bridgerton leads with reality, employing brilliant intimacy coordinators to choreograph the moments that emphasize emotional connection, consent, and the female gaze. Perhaps the only downside to these steamy scenes is the expectations they establish in our world! Damn you, Simon Basset (Regé-Jean Page)!

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

Perhaps one of the most important queer stories ever told, Fellow Travelers, based on the Thomas Mallon novel, is an emotionally charged, decades-spanning romance. The romantic thriller chronicles the secret love affair between two very different men, State Department official Hawkins Fuller (Matt Bomer) and idealistic congressional staffer Tim Laughlin (Jonathan Bailey), who meet in 1950s Washington, D.C. Beginning at the height of the McCarthy-era Lavender Scare in which the government actively hunted down and purged gay employees, Hawkins and Tim's clandestine relationship is tested by political paranoia, personal betrayal, and societal bigotry. With sweeping historical scope, Fellow Travelers preserves a crucial moment in history through explicit, frank intimacy driven by repressed desire, shifting power dynamics, and high-stakes historical danger.

Created by Ron Nyswaner, Fellow Travelers is a brilliant character-driven drama. The series does a stand-up job at presenting fully-fleshed individuals who must balance their personal desires with a fight to survive. These characters must guard every word and gesture in public. But then, behind closed doors, physical passion acts as an urgent release, heightening the power of intimacy. These moments range from tenderness, roughness, control, and surrender. As out actors, Bomer and Bailey have remarkable chemistry, understanding the gravity of these moments between Hawkins and Tim. While some scenes may be graphic, they're balanced with truth. These scenes will certainly make you blush, but they'll also hit your heart hard.

We're going to the cottage! Very few shows took the world by storm quite like Heated Rivalry. Created by Jacob Tierney and based on Rachel Reid's Game Changers book series, the Canadian romance series follows rival hockey superstars, Shane Hollander (Hudson Williams) and Ilya Rozanov (Connor Storrie). Over an eight-year span, the opposing captains engage in a secret, passionate romantic relationship off the ice while maintaining a fierce public rivalry on it. Together, they navigate intense professional pressures, family expectations, and the personal risks of keeping their love hidden in the high-stakes world of professional sports. Heated Rivalry examines toxic masculinity and the pressures of staying closeted in athletics while celebrating themes of identity, queer joy, and acceptance.

Heated Rivalry emerged as a breakout hit because it immediately dove deep into the intimacy. It pairs explicit, consent-driven romance with high-stakes tension and emotional vulnerability. Physical encounters act as an early language between Shane and Ilya while they struggle with verbal honesty and public expectations. As their bond shifts over time, so does their ability to communicate their true feelings. The scenes, staged by intimacy coordinator Chala Hunter, feel lived-in, playful, and emotionally grounded, balancing humor, awkwardness, and tenderness. Rather than lean into tragic tropes where intimacy leads to ruin, the narrative highlights the impact of true love, mutual respect, and emotional payoff. Beyond Shane and Ilya, Heated Rivalry crafts their own rivalry as the beautiful relationship between Scott Hunter (François Arnaud) and Kip Grady (Robbie G.K.) showcases a very different connection. Heated Rivalry became an instant obsession because the love is real. Come for the smut, stay for the love!

Based on the book series by Diana Gabaldon, the historical fantasy series Outlander is quite a compelling story. Developed by Ronald D. Moore, Outlander follows Claire Randall (Caitríona Balfe), a British World War II combat nurse who is mysteriously swept back in time from 1945 to 1743 Scotland. Forced to marry a chivalrous Highland warrior named Jamie Fraser (Sam Heughan) for protection, she falls deeply in love with him as she navigates a dangerous world. Torn between two eras and two men, Claire uses her modern medical skills to survive as she and Jamie become entangled in major historical upheavals, from the Jacobite Rising to the American Revolution. Outlander blends a time-travel mystery, historical drama, and an intense romance rooted in high-stakes survival with rich period pieces.

Outlander appealed to viewers looking for multi-genre drama handled with care. The eight-season series flourishes with its emotional depth and steamy romance, but it also does feature significant graphic violence, explicit torture, and sexual assault scenes. That said, those moments are counterbalanced by its intimacy. It focuses on desire and partnership. Claire openly initiates and enjoys sexual moments, while Jamie's devotion and low ego put his partner's feelings first. As Jamie and Claire grow older and face major trials and tribulations, the intimacy shifts from early, urgent passion to deeper trust and emotional understanding. With time travel, clan politics, and a lush Scottish locale setting the scene, Outlander masters heightened intimate tension in every moment the pair get together.

Inspired by 44 Chapters About 4 Men by BB Easton, the two-season erotic drama Sex/Life follows Billie Connelly (Sarah Shahi), a suburban wife and mother of two, who feels unfulfilled by the fading passion in her stable marriage to her husband, Cooper (Mike Vogel). Torn between her domestic life and memories of her wild past in New York City, Billie begins documenting her explicit fantasies about her bad-boy ex-boyfriend, Brad Simon (Adam Demos), in a private journey. When Cooper discovers the diary and Brad re-enters her life, Billie's past and present collide in a complicated love triangle. Sex/Life is a perfect series for those who love high-stakes melodrama, intense chemistry, and bold explorations of desire, identity, and marital complacency.

Sex/Life leads with a bold and provocative tone. With frequent nudity, candid conversations about desire, and steamy scenes until your heart's content, Sex/Life leans heavily into the crossroads of adult romance and soap opera intrigue. Beyond the fantasy elements, the series touches on losing the spark in long-term partnerships and balancing personal desires with necessary responsibilities. Created by Stacy Rukeyser, the show uses sex scenes to reflect shifting dynamics with Billie's "85/15" relationship tension, tapping into the friction between security and wilder impulses. At only 14 episodes, Sex/Life is a highly addictive, bingeable drama.

No show is as addictively frustrating as Tell Me Lies. Created by Meaghan Oppenheimer and based on the Carola Lovering novel, the three-season series follows the tumultuous, eight-year relationship between Lucy Albright (Grace Van Patten) and Stephen DeMarco (Jackson White) after they meet at Baird College. What starts as a typical campus romance quickly spirals into addiction, deceit, and emotional dependency. Jumping between their college years and the 2015 wedding of their friends Bree (Catherine Missal) and Evan (Brandon Cook), Tell Me Lies explores how Lucy and Stephen's destructive bond and dark secrets permanently alter the lives of everyone in their friend group. Tell Me Lies is a character-driven drama that leans heavily into the honest, messy relationships based on toxic pairings and psychological manipulation where sex is front and center.

While Tell Me Lies is filled to the brim with campus drama and steamy scenes, it's rooted in real emotional vulnerabilities. The show looks beyond surface-level romance to examine why people surrender their truth and self-respect to belong. The sexual scenes are presented as purpose-driven storytelling moments. Intimate scenes often highlight power shifts, emotional dependency, or shifting relationship dynamics rather than serving purely as filler fodder. Rather than reducing characters to simple heroes or villains, Tell Me Lies tackles insecurity, avoidance, and how small choices reshape an individual's personality and identity. The action escalates across each season, culminating in a shockingly obvious conclusion. If there’s one thing to take away from Tell Me Lies, it’s this: do not be like these people. None of them. They may have a redeemable quality or two, but they all display moments of manipulation that drop them into dangerous territory.

At the forefront of the early 2000s vampire obsession, True Blood was one of HBO's early smut spectacles of its heyday. The fantasy horror drama from Alan Ball follows Sookie Stackhouse (Anna Paquin), a telepathic waitress in the small town of Bon Temps, Louisiana, in a world where the invention of synthetic blood allows vampires to coexist publicly with humans. When Sookie falls in love with a mysterious 174-year-old vampire named Bill Compton (Stephen Moyer), her quiet life is upended as she is pulled into a dangerous underworld of vampire politics, werewolves, witches, and fairies. Blending dark romance, mystery, and Southern Gothic horror, True Blood explores the violent social clashes between humans and supernatural beings over assimilation and equal rights. Based on Charlaine Harris' The Southern Vampire Mysteries, True Blood balances pulpy escapism with masterful worldbuilding and distinct character development.

True Blood is the perfect balance of camp, romance, and grit. It shifts between murder mysteries, romantic entanglements, heavy gore, and self-aware humor without treating its melodrama as a distraction. True Blood leans into unapologetic sensuality, where desire is central to the plot. Vampire blood acts as both sustenance and an intoxicating, sensory trigger that blurs pain, pleasure, and power. It's the rush of intimate ecstasy that turns romance into a thrill ride. The story uses its setting's heat, humidity, and secrets to heighten the tension. At the same time, the concept of the "vampires out of the coffin" premise serves as a sharp debate on social and queer themes. From Sookie and Eric Northman's (Alexander Skarsgård) rival-to-romance tension-filled chemistry to Jason Stackhouse (Ryan Kwanten) and Jessica Hamby's (Deborah Ann Woll) chaotic, impulsive hookups, True Blood is never short of sex and intimacy. Even supernatural dream sequences are sexy spectacles! True Blood is not a show to watch with your parents around, no matter your age!

Babysitting Co-op – Trade Hours With Other Families Instead of Paying a Sitter

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

A babysitting co-op lets you trade hours of watching other families’ kids for hours of free sitting when you need it, for date nights or appointments. Join one near you or start one with five to 15 families you trust, track the hours in points, and cap how many points any family can owe.

Babysitters on Care.com post rates averaging about $20 an hour nationwide, so a four-hour date night costs about $80 in babysitting alone. With no family nearby, you pay that rate for every appointment and night out.

A co-op replaces that $80 with points. You earn one point for every hour you watch a child, and you spend one point for every hour someone watches one of yours. Points count across the whole group, so you can babysit for one family and spend the points with another.

Say you have two kids and watch one child from each of two other families for three hours on a Saturday afternoon. That earns six points, enough for a three-hour date night the next weekend.

To start one, invite five to 15 families who know each other, such as parents from your child’s preschool or your block. Then put the rules in writing before anyone babysits:

Book sitters and track points in a shared calendar and spreadsheet, or pay $15 a year per family for SittingAround. It sends each request for a sitter to the whole group, tracks every family’s points, and lists co-ops near you that you can ask to join.

If you can’t find five families, trade babysitting with one other family on alternate Saturday nights, or book one paid sitter for several families’ kids at one house and split the bill.

Search for a co-op near you, or start your own, on SittingAround.

Corero Network Security Introduces a Cloud DDoS Service, Extending Fast, Automatic Protection to Global Scale

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The new cloud and hybrid DDoS offering is to be delivered through a global alliance of Corero-Powered fulfillment partners which are "blended" together and  sold and operated through Corero's market leading technology toolsets.

LONDON, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Corero Network Security (AIM: CNS) (OTCQX: DDOSF), the distributed denial of service ("DDoS") protection specialists and champion of adaptive, real-time service availability, today announced its entry into the cloud DDoS market, giving customers the opportunity to buy cloud and hybrid DDoS protection from Corero.

The new DDoS protection service extends Corero Network Security's award-winning heritage in fast, automatic DDoS protection from on-premises to the cloud. Built on an industry-first, uniquely BLENDED model, it brings together the locations and capacity of a federated alliance of Corero-powered fulfillment partners. Together they form one global scrubbing footprint, with data centers in every major geography.

Corero now delivers a comprehensive blended DDoS solution for every need:

"Corero has long delivered exceptional DDoS mitigation on premises. Now we are extending that protection upstream," said Carl Herberger, CEO at Corero Network Security. "With our remarkable technology and a global network of partners, we now offer the highest performing hybrid and cloud-only DDoS scrubbing solutions. Organizations looking for a different choice in cloud DDoS now have one."

Unlike many cloud DDoS services, Corero's service is designed to strengthen its service provider partners rather than compete with them. Partners supply the scrubbing capacity, while Corero delivers the detection, protection, and service automation. The approach brings partners new customers and grows revenue from their existing DDoS services and infrastructure investments.

This new solution will be sold as a standalone Corero cloud backup service to new and existing customers, providing a comprehensive DDoS protection platform built upon Corero's market-leading SmartWall ONEâ¢ technology.

Exploring cloud or hybrid DDoS protection? Contact Corero before December 31, 2026, and receive up to six months free on a three-year engagement.

We Officially Ranked the 8 Best Netflix Shows of All Time

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As one of the biggest entertainment platforms in the world, Netflix has countless original series across practically every genre imaginable. From romance, sci-fi, horror, crime, the streaming giant has built a catalogue that has something for just about everyone. Of course, not every Netflix original is worth the binge, but every once in a while, the platform delivers a show that reminds us just how incredible episodic storytelling can be.

Here, we take a look at the best of the best in Netflix' vast library of shows. These series demonstrate the incredible range of storytelling and showcase great talents in front of and behind the cameras. Some will keep you glued to the screen with unexpected twists, while others will leave you thinking about their characters and themes for days. Spoiler alert: guilty pleasures or "second screen" shows most likely do not make this cut.

Based on John Steinbeck's novel, East of Eden follows the Trask family across multiple generations, starting with brothers Adam (Christopher Abbott) and Charles (Mike Faist) whose fortunes differ when their dad favors one over the other. Their lives changed when the mysterious Cathy Ames (Florence Pugh) arrives at their farm, influencing the decisions they make until later on in the future.

East of Eden is relatively new compared to the other series on this list but it is obvious that this show is one of the best things Netflix has put out in years. Zoe Kazan adapted this classic with care and respect, carrying her grandfather's legacy who adapted the third act of the novel with James Dean over 70 years ago. The thematic exploration of good and evil as well as the generational family dynamics are so satisfying and thorough. The show is further elevated by strong performances from the three leads, particularly Pugh. It also has scene-stealing performances from Hoon Lee, Ciaran Hinds and the new Hunger Games star Joseph Zada. It is undoubtedly one of the best literary adaptations ever made.

Set in the late 1970s, Mindhunter follows FBI agents Holden Ford (Jonathan Groff) and Bill Tench (Holt McCallany) as they research criminal profiling and psychology by interviewing serial killers. Working alongside psychologist Wendy Carr (Anna Torv), the two agents attempt to establish a new investigative discipline by examining the motivations and behaviors of some of the deadliest murderers.

Produced by David Fincher, who also directed seven out of 19 episodes in the show, Mindhunter has become a cult-favorite in Netflix' vast library. The show is moody and well-made, as expected of a Fincher production, but it's also slower-paced than the usual binge-worthy Netflix offerings, making it only a modest success. Anchored by the unlikely yet pleasant chemistry between Groff and McCallany, the show is fascinating for creating thrills out of conversations instead of the conventional crime spectacle. Unfortunately, because of its disappointing performance by the platform's standards, the show ended in its second season. As Fincher has exited Netflix, a continuation is most likely not happening.

In Money Heist or La Casa de Papel, a group of criminals is recruited by the Professor (Alvaro Morte) to carry out an almost impossible heist on the Royal Mint of Spain. The following seasons also see the group, with more additions to the team, back in action targeting the Bank of Spain's gold reserve to secure their lives away from the authorities once and for all. The ensemble cast includes Ursula Corbero, Itziar Ituño, Pedro Alonso, and Najwa Nimri.

With a total of five parts, Money Heist is Netflix's first international hit. The first two parts of the show are actually a re-edited version of the series' original run on a local TV channel in Spain. The striking imagery of Dali masks and red jumpsuits combined with incredible melodrama and intense action make it an immediate audience favorite. Parts three to five were produced by Netflix on a significantly larger budget and they paid off without losing the essence of the previous episodes. The performances from the ensemble cast are so engaging and memorable, resulting in many of the cast starring in high-profile projects afterwards. The series has spawned a Korean remake and a prequel spin-off focusing on Pedro Alonso's Berlin.

Arcane follows sisters Vi (voiced by Hailee Steinfeld) and Powder (Ella Purnell), who become entangled in the tensions in the prosperous city of Piltover and the struggling undercity of Zaun. The sisters eventually find themselves on opposite sides of an escalating conflict involving a new technology, with Powder transforming into the unpredictable Jinx and Vi attempting to reconnect with the person she once knew.

Before 2021, if someone said a show based on the popular game League of Legends is good, we would probably laugh it off. The joke's on us because Arcane is currently the highest-rated Netflix show on IMDb. The show boasts a stunning animation, inventive visual style, and exceptional voicework. The central relationship between the main characters drives the series and makes audiences invested in following their journey until the end. The themes that the show tackle are surprisingly complex and in-depth, examining class divisions and power. Arcane is not only one of Netflix' best shows, but also one of the finest video game adaptations of all time.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

The Queen's Gambit follows Beth Harmon (Anya Taylor-Joy), an orphaned chess prodigy who discovers her talent for the game while growing up in an orphanage. After being adopted by a troubled couple, Beth begins competing in chess tournaments, and eventually sets her sights on the international chess scene dominated by Soviet grandmasters. However, Beth also struggles with loneliness and drugs, which hamper her career.

Released during the pandemic lockdown period in 2020, The Queen's Gambit becomes a worldwide phenomenon and even responsible for introducing chess to a brand new generation. Adapted from the book and directed by Scott Frank, the series has a great production design, costumes, and direction. Anya Taylor-Joy shines as Beth who gives a complex performance as the troubled prodigy. She's joined by a strong ensemble cast that includes Moses Ingram, Harry Melling and Bill Camp. With its tightly written story, exceptional performances, elegant presentation, and satisfying character development, The Queen's Gambit is one of Netflix' near-perfect miniseries.

The Haunting of Hill House follows the Crain family, whose lives are changed after spending their childhood in a supposedly haunted mansion where they experienced a series of disturbing supernatural events. Years later, a tragedy brings the family back together and they are forced to confront the memories and traumas they have spent years avoiding. The series stars Victoria Pedretti, Oliver Jackson-Cohen, Henry Thomas, and Carla Gugino.

Created by modern horror master Mike Flanagan, The Haunting of Hill House is not just filled with empty scares. The series is filled with dread that goes beyond jump scares, building tension through unsettling imagery, hidden ghosts, and haunting sound design. It uses horror to explore grief, trauma, and family dysfunction. The series' biggest strength is its emotional storytelling, as each sibling's struggles feel like different responses to the same deeply painful childhood. While every episode is great, a couple of episodes, namely "The Bent-Neck Lady" and "Two Storms", are standouts for their story and technical achievements. Flanagan went on to create more exceptional horror shows, but this remains his best masterpiece.

The Crown chronicles the reign of Queen Elizabeth II, beginning with her marriage to Prince Philip and her unexpected ascension to the throne. Across six seasons, the series explores the royal family's personal relationships and key events that shape the Queen's standing as monarch, from the Suez Crisis to the whole ordeal with Princess Diana. The Queen is played by Claire Foy, Olivia Colman, and Imelda Staunton, with each of them taking the role for two seasons.

The Crown is one of Netflix's best and most fascinating shows because of its ability to turn historical events and political developments into intimate, character-driven drama. Series creator Peter Morgan is no stranger to royal family drama, having written The Queen with Helen Mirren years before. Its outstanding performances by seasoned actors and newcomers, particularly across the show's cast transitions, allow the characters to evolve naturally across decades. It is all further elevated by impeccable production design, costumes, and cinematography that make every season feel remarkably immersive.

Dark begins with the disappearance of a young boy in a small German town, which then brings four families into a generational mystery that spans decades. As the residents search for answers, they uncover a network of secrets involving time travel and the town's nuclear power plant. The story initially is set in 2019, but then jumps backwards and forwards to many timelines. The series stars Louis Hoffman, Lisa Vicari and Oliver Masucci, among many in the ensemble cast.

A convoluted story is often considered a drawback in films and series. However, for Dark, the more complicated the story becomes, the more intriguing and captivating it is. The sprawling, complicated premise created by Baran bo Odar and Jantje Friese is turned into a compelling, emotionally driven story. Its intricate timeline, intergenerational relationships, and constantly shifting revelations demand attention, but the series never relies on easy answers. The series trusts the audience completely to piece together the puzzle. Its gloomy atmosphere, haunting score, and meticulous storytelling become a staple of this show. Few science-fiction series are as ambitious in their construction, and even fewer manage to make a mystery as good as Dark.

A family saga with a supernatural twist, set in a German town where the disappearance of two young children exposes the relationships among four families.

SpaceX shares fell 2.25% in premarket trading on Wednesday to $122.98, down $2.83 from Tuesday’s close of $125.81, after a 5.65% drop in the previous session. Pressure built after Future Fund’s Gary Black said on X that investors should hold off until SpaceX falls below $100, which he expects once the company makes an all-stock offer for Tesla. ‘Ramayana’ Language App Cinedubs Named IFFI Accessibility Partner After Reaching More Than 100,000 Moviegoers in 42 Countries

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The mobile app that let more than 100,000 moviegoers in 42 countries hear “Ramayana” in a language of their choosing has signed on as accessibility partner for the International Film Festival of India (IFFI) 2026 in Goa.

The platform, Cinedubs, has brought its audio description and closed-caption technology to the festival, so that visually impaired and hearing-impaired audiences can watch films in theaters alongside everyone else. Related Stories Agatha Christie Series 'Tommy & Tuppence' Coming to Channel 5 in the U.K.

Nitesh Tiwari’s epic “Ramayana,” starring Ranbir Kapoor as Rama, Sai Pallavi as Sita and Yash as Ravana, opened worldwide on Nov. 5. Cinedubs allowed viewers to pick their own language no matter which version their local cinema was playing, and the company said that service added close to INR40 million ($425,000) to the film’s collections.

The system works through the Cinedubs app. Users download a soundtrack in the language they want and listen through headphones, while the app automatically locks the audio to the film on screen. The same technology carries audio description for visually impaired viewers and closed captions for hearing-impaired viewers.

“Being chosen as the Accessibility Partner for IFFI 2026 is a moment of immense pride for the entire Cinedubs team,” said Aditya Kashyap, founder of Cinedubs. “We firmly believe that cinema is a universal language and that neither linguistic barriers nor disabilities should prevent anyone from experiencing the power of storytelling.”

The company pitches its service to filmmakers, producers and distributors as a way to reach audiences they couldn’t previously get to and to earn extra revenue in the process.

Kashyap said the “Ramayana” numbers validated the company’s goal of making cinema borderless. “Accessibility is not just a social responsibility; it is also a significant opportunity for the global film industry to expand its audience base and unlock new revenue streams,” he said.

During IFFI, Cinedubs plans to meet with filmmakers, production houses, distributors and exhibitors about building a film business that is both more accessible and commercially viable. The company also sees a role in pushing Indian films further into international markets.

Built by Dubswork Mobile Pvt. Ltd., Cinedubs can be downloaded from both Apple’s and Google’s app stores.

For cinemas, the app’s multilingual soundtracks run without any changes to existing projection or sound systems.
