The Golden Rules of Affordable Family Travel, Illustrated by Mona Chalabi

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

My first Thanksgiving was a lavish 12-person dinner in Connecticut in 2014. I had moved to the US in March of that year and had planned on working from home until a friend generously invited me to go spend the day with her family; an act of unguarded openness that I’ve realized is one of the many things that differentiates Americans from Brits. As soon as we got off the train in Cambridge, Massachusetts, I realized that even though my friend lived in a nasty, overcrowded apartment in Brooklyn, she came from a family that had immense wealth. After dinner, as I helped wash plates in the kitchen, my friend’s mum asked her why she wouldn’t just move into the lovely building they had bought for her. She turned beet red and handed me another dirty plate without looking at me.

Take it from an immigrant: this holiday is full of weird contradictions. Of course, there’s the annual reminder of colonization which requires a careful forgetting of the people who have been colonized. And then there’s the financial aspect of it. At first blush, it seems like the least capitalist holiday of the year—there isn’t the expense of Christmas or Hanukkah gift-giving or the extravagance of Halloween candy. Yet the holiday also marks the start of one of the year's busiest travel seasons when families are taking great expense to reunite, vacation, and explore outside of their every day lives.

As we approach the last Thursday of November, with money very much on our minds, the below well-traveled parents share their hard-won wisdom on pulling off affordable family travel—including how to find treasure in New York City’s Chinatown without paying an arm and a leg, seek out free entertainment in the streets of Oaxaca, and embrace the joys of accessible public transportation in Europe. All of these stories say something about the wealth of the person writing it, whether or not they wanted to reveal that information. I’m obsessed with getting to the truth about someone’s class (it’s why I wrote my book on the subject), and so these family dispatches, which serve as equal parts advice and confession, are a treat I’ll come back to when I’m not busy washing a rich family’s plates. —Mona Chalabi, Pulitzer Prize-winning writer and illustrator

Keep reading for the tips and tricks of affordable family travel, road-tested by the experts and illustrated by Mona Chalabi.

I know the secret to parenting and I’m willing to share it: snacks. Snacks are everything—they’re an activity, a mood changer, and a window into culture for kids. Hit a local farmers market on your first day or if you’re somewhere truly out of their comfort zone even a local convenience store—like 7-Eleven in Tokyo—for something that can feel both familiar and novel. If your hotel room has a refrigerator you can store any pre-cut fruit that you buy, or opt for items that don’t require refrigeration like apples, bananas, and clementines. Bring a lightweight backpack, refill each morning, and watch the hangry meltdowns (mostly) vanish. —Dana Kravis, global executive director, CNT video

Last December in London, we stayed in a quaint Airbnb in Primrose Hill and explored different neighborhoods on foot. When our kids, then eight and three, got tired, we hopped on a double-decker bus and headed upstairs, hoping for front seats. Suddenly, getting home became an amusement park ride: Christmas lights, bustling streets, panoramic views. The kids loved it; as a photographer, I loved the vantage point. London caps daily bus fares when you use the same card or phone, and children under 11 ride free. We’ve done this in Singapore, too. Our rule: skip the sightseeing tour (or Uber) and make public transportation part of the family adventure. —Tanveer Badal, photographer

Breakfast is where family vacations are made, or broken. My moody teenagers can’t be pried out of bed no matter what city they wake up in, so instead of wringing my uncaffeinated hands and hoping to get us all on the same schedule, I just bring my own breakfast, coffee included. Whether in Paris, Rome, or a tent, you will find me hand-grinding my coffee beans, brewing a pour-over, and snacking on a pastry left over from the previous day while I peruse headlines. Avoiding the time and expense of a sit-down breakfast, and indulging in a bit of morning self-care, means we can focus on the adventures and meals we are actually going to remember. —Chris Morocco, food director of Bon Appétit and Epicurious

My parents and I meet in Aruba every winter for a family vacation that’s warm and relaxing when everyone else is cold and miserable. It’s a classic only child/parents trip where we spend most of our time eating lunch talking about where we’re going to eat dinner. We spend our days mostly just reading parallel to each other. I’ve never been an e-reader user, so I have to bring several physical books to read to get me through the week. The key is that my mom and I have some shared taste in books, so when we finish our own we can swap and save ourselves buying extra books (or lugging even heavier luggage through the airport). —Alison Leiby, comedian and writer

Finding creative ways to entertain wiggly one-year-olds with limited attention spans can be daunting, but it turns out all you need is a good window. From London to California, staying in rooms on low floors with windows overlooking lots of activity below has resulted in seemingly endless engagement—waving at commuters striding by at the Peninsula London, or golfers strolling a few feet away at the Ritz-Carlton Half Moon Bay? Bonus points if the room has curtains with electronic controls—that guarantees an extra hour of ooohs and aaahs, at least. —Sarah Khan, contributing editor

I have a thing for seeing “The World’s Biggest or Oldest,” and so here we were at Missouri’s Oldest Wooden Suspension Bridge. “Don’t tell your mom,” I said to my kids, because any bridge that was built from wood during the Civil War should be treated with caution—and, also, all good dad stories start that way. We started up the van, piled everyone in, and began the adventure. The wood cracked and echoed as we drove over the river. It sounded like a popping spinal cord. We swayed, and for a moment, I actually questioned myself. I should have just taken them to the park. Thankfully, I can now confidently say that Missouri’s oldest wooden suspension bridge can hold the weight of a mini-van. —Shannon Carpenter, humorist

New York is an expensive city and the best way to keep it cheap is to throw petty cash at it. Instead of buying tickets to an overpriced kid’s experience, I give my children a few twenty dollar bills and take them to Chinatown for a shopping spree. They love the atrocious arcade, and there are fake Labubus, squishy dumplings, a plastic “jade” green dragon that my son now keeps on his bedside, and a 'gold' necklace my daughter thinks is treasure. —Stephanie Danler, author

As a seasoned travel writer, I know that any hotel that’s gotten media coverage is often pushing $1,000/night. My trick for affordable family lodging is loading up Google maps, figuring out which area we’re going to stay in, and picking a cheerful place with good reviews in the $250 range. It’s almost never one you’ve heard of, but they’re always clean and cheerful, usually family-owned, and we always leave with big hugs and promises to keep in touch. That doesn’t happen at the Georges V. My kids (12 and 8) loved a converted windmill in Rome, a townhouse two blocks from the Rijksmuseum and—most recently—an orange grove near Siracusa where they spent so much time chasing the owner’s cats they even forgot Wi-Fi existed. A steal at any price! —Priyanka Mattoo, filmmaker and author

People tell you about a million things you “need” before your baby is born, but there's one that has come in the most handy, and I've had a true love/hate relationship with it since day one: A travel car seat. After a ton of research—some may say over-research—we bought a Maxi Cosi Romi. It's rated high for safety, but it's also easy to strap into a car and also an airplane. We always bring it on the airplane, and that's where the headache comes in. While the Maxi Cosi Romi is considered lightweight, it's also big and bulky, and the only way to get it through the airport and onto the plane is by wearing it in a special backpack. I'm pretty sure that I'm one of the most annoying people in any airport as I'm walking through it since I can't really see if I'm bumping into people in the TSA line or getting to the gate. The thing is that, after I considered the numbers, it's actually a really wise investment because most rental car places charge anywhere from $10 to $15 a day to rent a car seat, so we've already started saving money because of it. Now, that's about a hundred or more dollars we're not spending when we go out of town and bring the Maxi. It's wildly annoying and inconvenient, and I look like I'm wearing a jetpack as I'm walking through the airport, but it's worth it. —Jason Diamond, 'Figuring' family columnist at The Cut

I️ live in Brooklyn where people leave really good stuff out on the street. Once I️ found one of those giant extra storage containers you put on top of your car. I️ haven't found the time to figure out how to put it on top of the car; it's been taking up valuable space in my home for over a year. The other day, I️ found another one that looked even fancier. I️ have my eye on Block Island or Martha's Vineyard. —Liana Finck, cartoonist and author

From bustling markets to neighborhood festivals, I've learned that some of the best family entertainment happens in the streets. In Oaxaca, my four-year-old was mesmerized by calendas, raucous community processions with brass bands, towering puppets called monos de calenda, and rag-covered revelers known as tiliches. Paradegoers tossed my kid candy, fireworks exploded overhead, and the party raged late into the night. We didn't pay a peso. Before booking pricey family activities, research local festivals, celebrations, and public performances. Sometimes the most exciting show in town is happening right on the sidewalk. —Ashlea Halpern, contributing editor

On our last family vacation to Rajasthan, a passionfruit lassi from our five-star hotel nearly ruined the trip. We’re talking emergency roadside bathroom stops and several days of Zofran. We’ve been taking our kids—Vijay, Vik, and Meera—to Rajasthan since they were babies; they’re teenagers now. And we’ve learned one unexpected lesson: the sickest we’ve ever been in India was after eating at our five-star hotel. More than once.

So we’ve adopted a new philosophy: pack the right medicine and embrace street food. In Jaipur, Vineet Sharma, founder of Jaipur Walks, led us on a street food tour around the bustling market, introducing us to piping-hot pyaaz kachori and creamy, saffron-rich kulfi. His advice? Choose clean stalls with high turnover, and favor food cooked fresh and served hot over raw offerings. At busy stalls, food is often prepared continuously and served straight from the heat—sometimes a more reassuring bet than a meal at a luxury hotel or high-end restaurant. And if your stomach’s going to revolt, at least make the meal memorable. —Christine Chitnis, author and photographer

This may not be the most popular piece of advice, and I doubt it'll be the most obvious: take your kids overseas during school time. Amazon’s Lord of the Rings Series Was Close to Landing a Legendary Director

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Rings of Power, Amazon's Lord of the Rings streaming series now in its third season, has always aimed to be a prestigious affair. The show's budget is record-breaking, reportedly exceeding $1 billion. The show's prestige nearly got a further boost​​​​​​​, as lauded director David Fincher (Seven, The Social Network, Gone Girl) has just revealed he almost had a massive role in the project. It would have been Fincher's first true foray into fantasy, and now stands as just one more big "what if?" in the director's career.

Talking to host Joshua Horowitz on the Happy Sad Confused podcast to promote The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, Fincher offhandedly mentioned that he "got offered... what is the Lord of the Rings thing at Amazon?"​​​​​​​ Horowitz identified it as Rings of Power​​​​​​​ and asked if Fincher would have directed the pilot. The director revealed that, more surprisingly, he was asked to showrun the series. Fincher clarified that this all occurred before the series was picked up by Amazon. The reason he turned it down was that he felt the project was "an extension of a corporate interest."

Fincher went on to say that there is a "horrible constricting of the business right now," ​​​​​​​pointing to the merger between Paramount Skydance and Warner Bros. Discovery​​​​​​​ as an example of how it is "becoming more and more difficult to protect the individual voice in cinema." In keeping with that concern​​​​​​​, Fincher stated he wouldn't want to find himself ​​​​​​​in James Gunn's position as the newly ousted co-head of DC Studios​​​​​​​. However, Fincher also revealed​​​​​​​ during the interview that he almost worked with DC decades ago.​​​​​​​​​​​​​​

There was a time when David Fincher almost dipped his toe into the superhero film business. He revealed that ​​​​​​​in the 1990s, he was asked to pitch a Batman film, but the project went nowhere​​​​​​​. ​​​​​​​According to the director, his version would have been a more sinister take on the Batman world, which he thinks has much in common with Matt Reeves' The Batman.​​​​​​​ ​​​​​​​Fincher said his favorite actor to have played the role is Michael Keaton.​​​​​​​

Fincher also nearly entered the world of Star Wars.​​​​​​​ ​​​​​​​Fincher said that, before Lucasfilm was sold to Disney, he spoke with Kathleen Kennedy about taking the company public.​​​​​​​ "I was very interested in the idea of Star Wars," Fincher said. Ultimately, none of these projects came to fruition​​​​​​​.

A Landmark All-Lalanne Sale Is Heading to Sotheby’s

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Expected to fetch as much as $25 million, “Les Lalanne: Beyond Imagination” is the first-ever various-owner auction dedicated to Claude and François-Xavier Lalanne.

The market for Claude and François-Xavier Lalanne’s irreverent creations has been on a high lately. In April, a single wooly sheep sculpture by François-Xavier fetched $2.3 million. Over the summer, a quartet of his frog fountains smashed their $3.5 million estimate to achieve nearly $6.3 million. And the record for most expensive collectible design work belongs to a set of flower-adorned bronze mirrors crafted by Claude: They netted $33.5 million earlier this year, eclipsing François-Xavier’s $31.4 million hippo bar.

Now, ELLE Decor can exclusively share that Sotheby’s will host the first-ever auction dedicated to Les Lalanne, featuring pieces sourced from various collections. On December 8, more than 30 works by the late French artist duo will hit the block in “Les Lalanne: Beyond Imagination.” It is expected to fetch in the region of $25 million.

“Les Lalanne have become a global phenomenon, changing the way generations of collectors think about design,” says Jodi Pollack, chairman of 20th century design and major collections for the Americas. “A sheep can be a seat, a crocodile can be a console, and both can hold their own alongside the most important works in a collection.”

The collection spans everything from a gold-patinated bronze gorilla (estimated at $8–12 million) to a white marble owl (estimated at $4–6 million), and a flock of five sheep, including two lambs ($300,000–500,000 each). Highlights also include lesser-known motifs, such as a playful pair of bronze monkeys with deep-green patination that makes them almost look like jade ($800,000–1.2 million each), and two matching crocodile consoles ($700,000–1 million) that complement the famous chairs Claude created after obtaining a deceased crocodile from the Paris Zoo.

Those hoping to get an in-person glimpse at the creatures can see the menagerie starting December 2 at the Breuer building, Sotheby’s new Manhattan headquarters. To accompany the show, the auction house enlisted Milan-based artist Gaia Alari to devise custom illustrations for the catalogue, which reads like a fairy tale.

“The story begins in the catalogue and comes to life at the Breuer, where visitors will step into an enchanted winter garden and meet the cast,” says Pollack. “It’s our way of celebrating these works and the spirit in which they were conceived. We want visitors to look at familiar things as Claude and François-Xavier did and to see the extraordinary in the ordinary.”

“Les Lalanne: Beyond Imagination” will be on view at Sotheby’s New York from December 2 to 7. The live auction will take place on December 8, 2026, at Sotheby’s New York at 11 a.m..

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

Home-Price Growth Remains Outpaced as Inflation Continues to Rise

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As inflation remains elevated—with the latest Consumer Price Index (CPI) coming in hotter than economists’ expectations—“resilient” growth in home prices continues to be eroded.

The September S&P Cotality Case-Shiller Home Price Index was up 0.2% month-over-month, recording a 2.1% annual gain—the same as last month. This is compared to annual inflation readings of 3.4% in both the September CPI and Personal Consumption Expenditures price index.

“Nominal home prices remain resilient, but inflation is once again widening the gap between nominal and real housing returns,” said Rebecca Kaufman, associate director of Commodities at S&P Dow Jones Indices. She noted that this marks the “16th consecutive month in which U.S. home prices declined in real terms.”

Realtor.com® Senior Economist Anthony Smith also noted that this month’s data “reflects sales closing from July through September, a period when mortgage rates edged higher, with Freddie Mac’s 30-year fixed rate averaging 6.54% in July, near 6.7% in August, and 6.86% in September.”

The 10-City Composite was up 0.2% month-over-month to a 3.5% annual gain (down from 3.6% last month), while the 20-City Composite was up 0.1% month-over-month to a 2.6% annual gain (same as last month).

Kaufman also noted that this month’s report “revealed a widening gap between seasonal weakness and underlying price momentum,” as the monthly numbers pre-seasonal adjustment were declines across the board for the National, 10-City Composite and 20-City Composite Indices (-0.3%, -0.5% and -0.5%, respectively).

“That contrast suggests that some of the apparent monthly weakness reflects the normal fall slowdown rather than a broad deterioration in home prices,” she explained.

Smith agreed that this was reflective of “the normal fall slowdown,” as the “market’s momentum has weakened since September closed.”

Taking a look at the 20 cities that make up the index, Chicago once again led the pack (for the 6th consecutive month) with a 6.9% annual gain. Following behind were New York City at 5.8% and Miami at 4.6%.

Kaufman called out that Miami’s growth is “particularly notable,” as “after moving into fourth place among the 20 cities in July and August, it has now climbed into the top three, a sharp reversal from its comparatively weak performance in 2025.”

In terms of declines, Seattle was again the leader for the 4th consecutive month with an annual fall of 1.9%. Denver (-1.0%) and Dallas (-0.9%) followed behind.

For the picture ahead, Smith said that the “rate and demand backdrop has become more challenging since September closed,” as mortgage rates are now above 7% and inflation remains fairly elevated—with “expectations for a Fed rate hike in December.”

“If financing costs stay elevated into the winter, the plateau in price growth could give way to renewed softening, particularly in supply-heavy Southern and Western markets,” he continued.

Claudia Larsen is a senior editor for RISMedia.

SpaceX launched a Falcon 9 rocket carrying a classified US Space Force satellite from Vandenberg Space Force Base in California on Tuesday. Gifted Stock Carries Your Cost Basis – Give It to Someone in the 0% Bracket

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

To give stock, transfer the shares to the recipient’s brokerage account or a child’s custodial account. Your cost basis, what you paid, goes with them, so the recipient owes tax on your gain when they sell. Give appreciated shares to someone whose low income means 0% tax on long-term gains, and sell losing shares yourself to deduct the loss.

The gain you built is then taxed at the recipient’s rate, not yours, so the right recipient can owe nothing.

Say shares you bought years ago for $2,000 are worth $10,000 now, and you pay 15% on long-term gains, those on investments held over a year. Selling them yourself costs $1,200 in federal tax on the $8,000 gain.

Give them to your daughter in her first full year of work after college. The time you’ve held them counts as hers, so she gets long-term rates at once. If her taxable income, the gain included, stays low enough for the 0% long-term rate, she owes no federal tax on it. The kiddie tax limits this for anyone under 18, and for 18-year-olds and full-time students under 24 whose earnings cover no more than half their support. It taxes their investment income above a small yearly amount at a parent’s rate when that’s higher.

To make the gift, you’ll need the recipient’s brokerage name and account number for your broker’s online gift tool or form. For a child, open a custodial account, often called a UTMA or UGMA account, in their name. The shares become the child’s the day you give them, and the custodian manages them until the child comes of age.

Giving over $19,000 in a year to one person other than your spouse means filing a gift tax return, but you owe tax only once your lifetime gifts pass a multimillion-dollar exemption. Shares given to a charity or a donor-advised fund leave no one owing tax on the gain, since charities pay no income tax. Heirs who inherit shares in a taxable account get the shares’ value at death as their basis, so the gain before then goes untaxed.

Never give shares worth less than you paid. Neither of you can deduct the drop that came before the gift. Sell them, deduct the loss on your own return, and give the cash.

Before the transfer, open your broker’s cost basis page and send only the batches of shares that have gained.

Delta Air Lines Set To Reveal New 17-Hour Nonstop Route Next Week: See Map Now

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Delta Air Lines has fallen behind United Airlines in terms of its transpacific network in recent years, but the Atlanta-based carrier has started to close that gap. This year, it reinstated flights between Los Angeles and Hong Kong, and next year it is reinstating its nonstop services between Seattle and Tokyo Narita, and also launching flights to Manila.

The airline is now preparing to announce yet another addition to its network next week. The SkyTeam member is hosting a major event in Seattle on December 2, where it is set to unveil at least one new route. It is widely expected to be Singapore, which it last served in 2019 via Tokyo Narita International Airport (NRT).

Delta has been planning a return to the Lion City for some time. As Simple Flying reported in August, the airline's Chief Commercial Officer, Joe Esposito, confirmed that Singapore Changi Airport (SIN) was part of its future network plans. There has since been further evidence that the service could launch as early as next year.

Evidently, according to View From The Wing, the Atlanta-based airline identified Singapore as one of its new markets for 2027 in a July 29 presentation to a Pacific air traffic coordination group. The airline stated in the presentation, “New markets announced for 2027 are Manila and Singapore.”

The service is expected to operate from the airline's West Coast hub at Seattle-Tacoma International Airport (SEA) and would have an estimated block time of around 17 hours. Delta will likely deploy its Airbus A350-900 on the route, although the larger A350-1000 could eventually take over the service.

Based on what is known so far, the service could launch in the summer or fall of 2027. Delta previously indicated that its Singapore service would follow the launch of its Los Angeles–Manila route. The latter is scheduled to commence on March 28, 2027, initially operating three times weekly before increasing to daily on June 7.

Singapore was once part of an extensive connecting network through Tokyo that Delta inherited from Northwest Airlines. When the carrier decided to withdraw from the Singapore market, it cited financial pressure on the route. Growing nonstop capacity between the US and Singapore, stronger brand loyalty towards local carriers, and competition from Star Alliance members at Changi made it increasingly difficult for Delta to compete.

But the proposed service would put Delta in direct competition with Singapore Airlines. According to scheduled data from aviation analytics provider Cirium, the Singaporean flag carrier operates five weekly flights between Seattle and Singapore. It also dominates the wider US–Singapore market, as noted above in the table. Meanwhile, United Airlines is currently the only US carrier serving Singapore nonstop, operating twice daily from San Francisco.

Only 2 airports in the Pacific are in Delta Air Lines' plans. Is this really enough, or is this actually a global trend in development?

It is interesting to see Delta expanding its presence in Asia. Its overall capacity in Asia is up around 5.8% this year compared to 2025. As noted earlier, there have been several interesting developments in its transpacific network. In June, the airline reinstated daily flights between its Southern California hub and Hong Kong International Airport (HKG) after decades of absence on the route.

Notably, it also marked its return to Hong Kong for the first time since 2018. From March 27, 2027, the airline will resume daily flights between Seattle and Tokyo Narita for the first time since March 2020, complementing its existing service to Tokyo Haneda Airport (HND). Elsewhere, it will make its debut in the Philippines market.

While not part of its regular network, the SkyTeam member has also scheduled a handful of nonstop flights to and from Las Vegas Harry Reid International Airport (LAS) for the upcoming Consumer Electronics Show (CES). These include flights between Las Vegas and Hong Kong on January 4 and 10, and between Las Vegas and Taipei on January 5 and 10.

Here's How Much Of A Flight Airline Pilots Actually Hand-Fly

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

guys_who_shoot | Shutterstock, Simple Flying

On a typical commercial flight, airline pilots only spend a small proportion of their time physically controlling the aircraft. Pilots normally hand-fly takeoff and part of the initial climb, while the autopilot handles most of the flight until approach. According to a 2026 report by the International Air Transport Association (IATA), commercial aircraft operate with automation engaged for approximately 90% of the time.

On a long-haul flight, a pilot may spend just a few minutes controlling a Boeing 787 or Airbus A350, although operating procedures and weather conditions can influence this substantially. Moreover, a pilot not physically moving the controls does not mean the aircraft is operating without human supervision. The crew remains responsible for managing the flight path, monitoring the automated systems, and taking over whenever necessary.

Airborne manual flying normally starts with takeoff, which remains a pilot-controlled maneuver on conventional commercial airplanes. Taxiing is generally manual as well, but is not included in the airborne flight time. The pilot maintains directional control, rotates, and establishes the initial climb. Although various systems can manage engine power, this should not be confused with the aircraft performing an automatic takeoff. The autopilot can only be engaged once the aircraft has reached its approved minimum engagement height.

After departure, the crew can generally engage the autopilot relatively early, sometimes only a few hundred feet above the ground. According to IATA's 2020 Aircraft Handling and Manual Flying Skills Report, many surveyed pilots reported engaging it around 400 or 500 feet above ground level, while others preferred to continue flying manually through 5,000 or even 10,000 feet. The exact altitude depends on the aircraft and airline.

Once engaged, the autopilot can handle most of the climb, cruise, and descent, following the route and altitude instructions managed by the crew. On arrival, the pilot may disconnect it early during a visual approach or keep it engaged until shortly before landing. The final flare and touchdown are normally performed manually, although suitably equipped aircraft can also carry out automatic landings under the appropriate conditions.

Estimates of how much time airline pilots spend hand-flying differ substantially, partly because there is no universal operating procedure. Back in 2011, airline captain Rory Kay, co-chair of a Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) advisory committee on pilot training, said that pilots were using automation "for all but approximately three minutes of a flight".

Another committee member, captain Bob Coffman, provided an even lower estimate for some regional airline operations. According to Coffman, pilots at commuter airlines might fly with the autopilot disengaged for approximately 80 seconds during a typical two-hour flight. Both estimates are historical rather than current industry averages.

Other pilots take a somewhat different approach. In an interview published by Johnny Jet, airline pilot Spencer Marker explained that most airline pilots spend ten minutes or less manually controlling their aircraft. However, he personally preferred to hand-fly departures until reaching 10,000 feet and disconnect the autopilot approximately five miles from the runway when weather and operating conditions allowed.

The length of the flight also makes a significant difference. Ten minutes represents almost 17% of a one-hour flight, but only 1.7% of a ten-hour service. On an ultra-long-haul operation, such as Qantas' nonstop Perth (PER) - London (LHR) route, the proportion can become even smaller.

From filling out paperwork to maintaining communication and constantly checking the instruments, the pilots are kept busy on the flight deck.

Although the autopilot physically controls the aircraft for much of a flight, it is not an independent system that simply receives a destination and makes every decision. Modern airliners use several connected systems, including the flight management system, flight director, autopilot, and automatic thrust controls. These each perform different functions, and the crew must ensure that the appropriate modes are selected and that the aircraft is following its intended flight path.

There is also an important difference between flying manually and flying without any assistance. A pilot can disconnect the autopilot while keeping the flight director engaged, which provides guidance on the primary flight display. Automatic thrust can also remain active while the pilot controls the aircraft's pitch and bank. Flying using basic instrument indications without flight director guidance, known as raw-data flying, demands additional proficiency.

During cruise, the pilots continue monitoring navigation, weather, fuel consumption, aircraft systems, and instructions from air traffic control. Changes to the assigned route or altitude must be correctly entered and verified. Automation also helps distribute workload between the pilot flying and pilot monitoring, especially during departures and approaches where both pilots may be managing several tasks simultaneously.

The time spent holding the controls therefore provides little indication of the crew's overall workload. Even with autopilot engaged, the pilots must decide whether the selected automation remains appropriate.

The pilot landing an ultra-long-haul A350 needs two consecutive hours of rest in the second half of the flight.

Airlines can take remarkably different approaches to manual flying. Some encourage pilots to use the highest available level of automation throughout most of the flight, while others explicitly allow manual flying whenever weather, air traffic, and cockpit workload permit. Manual flying opportunities therefore depend heavily on each airline's standard operating procedures (SOP).

IATA's 2020 survey, which received 5,650 responses overall, found that only 36% of the 5,241 pilots answering the relevant question said their airline allowed and supported manual flying without limitations. A separate question attracted 3,452 responses, of which approximately 37% admitted hesitating to disengage the autopilot and take manual control. Some respondents also feared criticism for hand-flying even where procedures permitted it.

Complex departures, congested airspace, and reduced visibility can increase workload, making automation the safer option. Some approaches and airspace procedures also require automation for precise navigation. An airline therefore needs to balance manual flying opportunities against the operational conditions of each individual flight.

IATA's survey also found differences during landing, with many pilots disconnecting the autopilot around 1,000 feet above the runway when conditions permitted. Others did so earlier or at the minimum altitude allowed by the relevant procedure. An approach flown with the autopilot engaged is not automatically an autoland; in most cases, pilots still take control before touchdown.

Modern airliners are incredibly automated, but pilots remain fully responsible for every decision during a flight. What do they actually do?

One particularly interesting difference concerns the type of flying performed by the crew. Short-haul pilots might operate several sectors on the same day, giving them multiple opportunities to practice takeoffs and landings. Long-haul pilots, on the other hand, can spend most of a working day on one flight, and augmented crews may share the workload during particularly long services. Consequently, each pilot may perform far fewer takeoffs and landings.

A 2017 study surveyed more than 1,500 commercial pilots about manual flying and airline procedures. Only 35.5% of long-haul pilots agreed that they had sufficient opportunities to hand-fly, compared with 79.9% of pilots operating short- and medium-haul flights. Pilots flying aircraft such as the Boeing 777, Airbus A380, and Airbus A330/A340 were among those reporting the fewest opportunities.

The same study also identified a clear difference between airline cultures. Among pilots working in supportive procedural environments, 68% felt they had sufficient opportunities to maintain their manual flying skills during normal operations. That figure fell to just 26% among pilots whose airlines imposed more restrictive procedures.

The difference is also not simply explainable by aircraft age or technology. United Airlines continues operating its Boeing 767 fleet even as newer widebodies enter service, and both generations rely extensively on automation. Accumulating flying hours on long sectors does not necessarily mean practicing basic aircraft handling.

While most landings follow a stable approach, almost all unstable approaches are followed by landing.

Concerns about reduced manual flying opportunities have been raised by aviation regulators for well over a decade. The Associated Press reported in 2011 that a draft FAA study had examined 46 accidents and major incidents, alongside hundreds of voluntary reports and observations from more than 9,000 flights. It found problems involving manual aircraft handling or automated flight controls in more than 60% of the accidents and 30% of the major incidents reviewed. These were findings from a selected sample, rather than percentages applying to all airline accidents.

The FAA subsequently issued Safety Alert for Operators 13002 in 2013, encouraging airlines to provide suitable opportunities for manual flying. A further safety alert in 2017 called for manual flying proficiency to be reinforced through both training and normal line operations. It recommended manual instrument departures and arrivals, upset recovery, and training across different automation modes.

The issue has not disappeared. In its 2026 flight path management report, IATA identified manual handling as an important safety concern and called for training and airline policies that develop both manual and automated flight path management skills. Full-flight simulators can reproduce unreliable airspeed, unusual attitudes, and other situations unsuitable for practice on commercial flights.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

However, simulators are not necessarily a complete substitute for line flying. Research found that nearly 80% of surveyed pilots disagreed that simulator training alone was sufficient to maintain their manual flying skills. The aim is not to minimize autopilot use, but to preserve safe opportunities for pilots to control their aircraft in normal operations.

The Vogue Book Club’s Next Read Is ‘Mrs. Dalloway’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

What a lark! What a plunge! Next up on the Vogue Book Club’s reading list is none other than Mrs. Dalloway, the classic 1925 novel by Virginia Woolf.

Our selection comes near the end of a year crowded with Woolf adaptations: Virginia Woolf’s Night and Day, based on her 1919 novel, directed by Tina Gharavi and written by Justine Waddell, and two retellings of Mrs. Dalloway—Rachel Rose’s The Last Day, starring Alicia Vikander, Victoria Pedretti, and Wagner Moura, and Clarissa from Arie Esiri and Chuko Esiri, starring Sophie Okonedo, David Oyelowo, and Ayo Edebiri.

In the September 15, 1925, issue of Vogue, our reviewer, Berenice Claire Skidelsky, compared the prose style employed in Mrs. Dalloway—fleet and fluid, drifting in and out of the minds and memories of its characters—to James Joyce’s in Ulysses, published three years earlier. “Both books have as their substance the odyssey of a group of persons through a day—not only their outward circumstances, but the intricate twistings and windings of their thoughts and the interplay of their lives, one with another,” Skidelsky wrote. “Both recognize the importance of the nuance in human significances; and both get arresting effects in an extremely novel manner.”

On the subject of Mrs. Dalloway’s actual plot—Mrs. Dalloway prepares for a party while a veteran of World War I suffers from post-traumatic stress—Skidelsky noted, dryly: “No summary of the story can be given, for the simple reason that there isn’t any story.” Yet that isn’t, strictly speaking, a criticism. “A series of incidents move with sequence and with charm into the formation of a unity that has novelty and strength to recommend it,” she continued. “A faintly melancholy consciousness of the evanescence of youth, and, indeed, of life itself, that rings like an echo ever and again throughout the book…adds poignancy of appeal for readers to whom the essence of life reflected in literature is of more moment than a story.”

Befitting, in a way, the book’s unusual structure, the Vogue Book Club is varying its format for Mrs. Dalloway. Instead of publishing a succession of stories on a weekly basis, we’ve declared December 14–18 Woolf Week here at Vogue. (Clarissa is in limited theaters December 18.) Every day that week will yield new stories about Mrs. Dalloway and the life and work of Woolf more broadly.

Eager to follow along? Those who don’t already have a dog-eared copy of Mrs. Dalloway at home can pick up a (discounted!) paperback here. Then let the schedule below guide your reading—and when you’re done, meet us here (and on the Vogue app!) for Woolf Week. See you soon!

Day 1: The beginning (“Mrs. Dalloway said…”) to approx. page 30 (…“so she rocked: so she shivered.”)

Day 2: Approx. page 30 (“Millicent Bruton, whose lunch parties…”) to approx. page 64 (“He never saw her again.”)

Day 3: Approx. page 64 (“It was awful, he cried…”) to approx. page 90 (“He gave in.”)

Day 4: Approx. page 90 (“Nothing could rouse him”) to approx. page 120 (“And he went.”)

Day 5: Approx. page 120 (“How like him!”) to approx. page 151 (“So that was Dr. Holmes”)

Day 6: Approx. page 151 (“One of the triumphs of civilization…”) to approx. page 168 (“…hold themselves upright.”)

Day 7: Approx. page 168 (“Gently the yellow curtain…”) to end

Prime Video is confirming the return of 'The Lord of the Rings' TV series ahead of the third season finale airing.

Prime Video’s journey into the Second Age of Middle-earth will continue.

The streamer confirmed Tuesday that The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power has been officially renewed for a fourth season.

In addition, amid some online speculation, the company confirmed showrunners J.D. Payne and Patrick McKay will be back as well.

Payne, Patrick McKay and the entire creative team. We look forward to bringing fans even deeper into the rich world of Tolkien’s Second Age.”

IMPORTANT SAFETY INFORMATION BOTOX® Cosmetic may cause serious side effects that can be life threatening. Get medical help right away if you have any of these problems any time (hours to weeks) after injection of BOTOX® Cosmetic:

BOTOX® Cosmetic dosing units are not the same as, or comparable to, any other botulinum toxin product.

There has not been a confirmed serious case of spread of toxin effect when BOTOX® Cosmetic has been used at the recommended dose to treat frown lines, crow’s feet lines, forehead lines, and/or platysma bands.

BOTOX® Cosmetic may cause loss of strength or general muscle weakness, vision problems, or dizziness within hours to weeks of receiving BOTOX® Cosmetic. If this happens, do not drive a car, operate machinery, or do other dangerous activities.

Serious and/or immediate allergic reactions have been reported, including itching, rash, red itchy welts, wheezing, asthma symptoms, or dizziness or feeling faint. Get medical help right away if you are wheezing or have asthma symptoms, or if you become dizzy or faint.

Do not receive BOTOX® Cosmetic if you are allergic to any of the ingredients in BOTOX® Cosmetic (see Medication Guide for ingredients); had an allergic reaction to any other botulinum toxin product such as Myobloc (rimabotulinumtoxinB), Dysport (abobotulinumtoxinA), Xeomin (incobotulinumtoxinA), Jeuveau (prabotulinumtoxinA-xvfs), Daxxify (daxibotulinumtoxinA-lanm), or Letybo (letibotulinumtoxinA-wlbg); or have a skin infection at the planned injection site. This list may not include all available botulinum toxin products.

Tell your doctor about all your muscle or nerve conditions, such as ALS or Lou Gehrig’s disease, myasthenia gravis, or Lambert-Eaton syndrome, as you may be at increased risk of serious side effects, including difficulty swallowing and difficulty breathing, from standard doses of BOTOX® Cosmetic.

Tell your doctor about all your medical conditions, including surgery or plans to have surgery on your face, trouble raising your eyebrows, drooping eyelids, any other abnormal facial change, are pregnant or plan to become pregnant (it is not known if BOTOX® Cosmetic can harm your unborn baby), or are breastfeeding or plan to (it is not known if BOTOX® Cosmetic passes into breast milk).

Tell your doctor about all the medicines you take, including prescription and over-the-counter medicines, vitamins, and herbal supplements. Using BOTOX® Cosmetic with certain other medicines may cause serious side effects. Do not start any new medicines until you have told your doctor that you have received BOTOX® Cosmetic in the past.

Tell your doctor if you have received any other botulinum toxin product in the last 4 months; have received injections of botulinum toxin such as Myobloc, Dysport, Xeomin, Jeuveau, Daxxify, or Letybo in the past (tell your doctor exactly which product you received); have recently received an antibiotic by injection; take muscle relaxants; take an allergy or cold medicine; take a sleep medicine; or take aspirin-like products or blood thinners.

Other side effects of BOTOX® Cosmetic include dry mouth; discomfort or pain at the injection site; tiredness; headache; neck pain; and eye problems, including double vision, blurred vision, decreased eyesight, drooping eyelids and eyebrows, swelling of eyelids, and dry eyes.

Approved Uses BOTOX® Cosmetic is a prescription medicine that is injected into muscles and used to temporarily improve the look of moderate to severe forehead lines, crow’s feet lines, frown lines between the eyebrows, and vertical bands connecting the jaw and neck (platysma bands) in adults.

For more information, refer to the Medication Guide or talk with your doctor.

To report a side effect, please call Allergan Aesthetics at 1-800-678-1605.

Measure Your Pet’s Food – Extra Scoops Mean Bigger Food & Vet Bills

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Measure your pet’s meals with a kitchen scale or a level measuring cup, and feed the label’s amount for the weight your pet should be, not its current weight. Count treats as part of the day’s food. Eyeballed portions tend to run big, so the bag empties sooner and the extra pounds raise vet bills.

Those extra pounds are common. In the Association for Pet Obesity Prevention’s 2022 survey, veterinary professionals found 59% of dogs and 61% of cats overweight or obese.

Even a cup or scoop leaves room for error. When University of Guelph researchers had 100 dog owners measure set amounts of kibble, their portions ran from 48% too little to 152% too much, and the smallest amount, a quarter cup, gave them the most trouble.

Say the feeding chart calls for a third of a cup a day and your cat gets half a cup. That’s 50% more than the chart’s amount, so a bag meant to last two months is gone in about six weeks and your cost per day rises by the same 50%. The American Animal Hospital Association’s nutrition guidelines link obesity to arthritis and diabetes. In Banfield Pet Hospital’s 2017 report, owners of overweight dogs spent 17% more on health care and 25% more on medications than owners of healthy-weight dogs.

Weigh meals on a kitchen scale, which the same guidelines rate as more accurate than measuring cups. Weigh one level cup of the food once, and a third-cup meal is a third of that weight. With a cup, pick one the size of the portion and level it off.

Use the chart’s amount for your pet’s ideal weight, which your vet can tell you, and treat it as a starting point. The guidelines base a pet’s calories on its ideal weight and warn that the amounts on labels can run high.

Keep treats to 10% of the day’s calories, the guidelines’ limit, or use pieces of the day’s measured kibble as treats. A bowl that stays full all day can’t be measured, so switch to set meals.

Then check your pet’s shape. You should be able to feel its ribs but not see them, and see a waist behind them from above. If you have to press to find the ribs, feed a little less, whatever the chart says.

Studio Gang completes women's leadership campus in Wisconsin

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Architecture practice Studio Gang has completed a women’s conference centre overlooking Geneva Lake in Wisconsin, with three irregularly shaped buildings informed by the site’s oak forest.

Located outside of Chicago on a lakeside hill, the Women’s Leadership Center contains meeting spaces for established and emerging women in leadership roles to gather and “ideate solutions to complex issues within their industries”.

“Women leaders spend much of their careers in rooms that weren’t designed with them in mind,” said the center’s founder Ann Drake, former CEO of supply chain provider DSC Logistics. “I wanted to build one that was.”

Studio Gang said key elements of the design were sustainability – with strategies such as a mass timber structure – and responding to the ecology of the site through gently curving volumes and wide-open windows.

“We designed the Women’s Leadership Center to respect and learn from its surrounding forest ecology while allowing community, leadership, and collaborative problem-solving to thrive,” said Studio Gang founding partner Jeanne Gang.

“Locally sourced materials, mass timber, and low-carbon concrete minimise the buildings’ carbon footprint, while the landscape helps to repair the site’s ecology and enhance its biodiversity.”

The campus contains three main buildings — the circular Council building that functions as the main meeting space, the Lodge, which is made of three interconnected volumes, and the Cabin, a smaller building that contains accommodations.

White oak wood harvested on-site, a dark wood cladding and natural Wisconsin stone were used throughout all three buildings to “reflect the tones and textures of the forest”, according to the studio.

The Council building is arranged around a central courtyard and garden that doubles as a rainwater-collection site.

Its rooms, including a larger lecture hall and meeting room, fan outwards around the courtyard and are lined with floor-to-ceiling windows, while a heavy timber structure creates a series of skylights at the entrance.

A walkway leads out the back of the Council building towards the Lodge. The building contains more casual meeting and socialising spaces, such as a Great Room arranged around a fireplace.

It also contains a mass-timber structure above this space, which is lined with skylights. Outdoor terraces overlook Geneva Lake, while the building’s green roof contains a “coffee nook” and patios for seating.

Tucked off into a corner of the site, the Cabin building contains several bedrooms across two levels, as well as utility spaces such as a laundry room.

Its lower level contains a dining area and living area, which double as additional, more intimate gathering spaces.

Fully accessible ramps interconnect all three structures and lead down to the water bank, where they end in a pier.

Elsewhere, Studio Gang recently completed an open-air theatre in upstate New York and a Denver hotel.

Amar Dedic set for extended spell on the sidelines after Newcastle latest - verdict

THE INSIGHT Harri utilises a network of officiating and industry contacts to deliver verified, expert analysis of refereeing decisions and PGMOL policy. He provides the regulatory clarity behind the controversy to ensure fans get the full picture.

Matthias Jaissle finally got the best out of his Newcastle United squad in a thrilling 2-2 draw against Arsenal at St James' Park.

The Gunners were forced to come from behind to salvage a point on Saturday, 21 November, doing so thanks to Noni Madueke.

After Eberechi Eze's opener, Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw hit back in the second half, and the Magpies were unfortunate not to keep the lead.

Jaissle certainly made the hard calls during the game, including a triple substitution at half-time.

He sacrificed Sean Steur, Jacob Murphy and Amar Dedic during the break, with the latter picking up an early booking against Arsenal.

All three were deserving of the axe at St James' Park, and Dedic could find himself stuck on the bench after his showing.

As well as his early caution, Dedic won just 50 per cent of his ground and aerial duels, was dribbled past once, and completed just six passes [FotMob].

He was replaced by Tino Livramento at the break, with the star full-back returning from injury for the first time since mid-September.

While it was only 45 minutes under his belt, the 24-year-old's return to action could now keep Dedic out of the team for the foreseeable future.

Of course, Jaissle won't want to risk Livramento picking up yet another problem, but there is a clear gap is quality between the two players.

The two will have to battle it out for game time in the upcoming clashes against Brighton, Manchester United and Sunderland.

The half-time changes certainly don't spell good news for Steur or Murphy, and there are players on the bench waiting to take their places.

Jacob Ramsey deserves another start, while Newcastle fans will be desperate for Anthony Elanga to get back to his best form.

If Jaissle had another goalkeeper at his disposal, and not Nick Pope, it might even be time to give Lukas Hornicek a rest.

With Ewen Jaouen still recovering from injury, though, the 24-year-old will simply have to bounce back against the Seagulls on Sunday, 29 November.

Vampire Therapist review - “The problems of unlife aren't so black and white.”

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

For over a century now, the vampire has fascinated humanity. From books to films, television and yes, even video games, the enigma of an undead bloodsucker that nonetheless has the wisdom of the ages is a contradiction we all seek to understand. Vampire Therapist isn’t concerned with tragic backstories or merciless sadism but with how to move past them.

In Vampire Therapist, you play undead cowboy Samuel Walls, who is invited to Germany by the three-thousand-year-old Andromachos. For some devious scheme? No, but to help other people. Vampires, that is. Because, as Samuel Walls, you are the one and only Vampire Therapist. And God help you, because these bloodsuckers have some problems.

How can I help you tonight? Vampire Therapist is a visual novel with puzzle elements where, as Walls, your job is to - surprise, surprise - provide psychological aid to your undead kin. To do so, you listen to their problems and aim to catch them in mental contradictions which hold them back from improving or seeing reality.

It can seem a little gamified, but there is definitely a fascinating deductive element to catching your client doing things like Labeling or Disqualifying the Positive, all of which seem to be real therapy terms, but which are explained in a digestible format. The focus is not on catching out your clients, but on taking advantage of these contradictions to show them where they can manage their problems.

You’ll also get to make occasional decisions on what to say or do, which can shift the story in different directions. The focus is mainly on the psychological element, and that in itself is enough to carry the whole game. In fact, I was a little miffed when it got interrupted by a blood-drinking minigame, not least because it was a little janky in the build which I played.

Whenever you have an issue, you can look back at your notes to study them for references to your client’s case files, as well as Sam’s notes that point to the issues they have. You can also look up definitions of the psychological contradictions, including some handy examples. Admittedly, I missed these on my playthrough, but they are there if you’re the journal-reading type, and Andromachos will be on hand to guide you through initial sessions anyway. The Looks and Sound of Death In visual terms, Vampire Therapist is your typical visual novel. You have character sprites with occasional shifts in pose and expression, while vivid backgrounds show which scene you’re in. The art style is a decent middle ground between realistic and stylised, meaning even the more stereotypical vampires, such as the half-century-old Renaissance aristocrat, aren’t boiled down to simple caricatures.

The music in Vampire Therapist is pretty simple, consisting of the main theme and occasional background noises. I did find the track that plays in the vampire club, which your patron Andromachos uses as his base of operations, a little repetitive, which can be an issue since you revisit it pretty frequently.

The voice acting, however, is undoubtedly a standout. Naturally, Matthew Mercer provides his vocal talent as incidental character Reinhard, and also appears later on in the full game. But Cyrus Nemati is a standout as Sam Walls, who provides cosy mix of stereotypical gravely cowboy and surprisingly self-aware, world-wise wanderer. What’s the story, morning glory? The actual narrative of Vampire Therapist is pretty engaging, and what we find out about Sam Walls, Andromachos and your clients such as Doctor Drayne (yes, really) and Isabella d’Este paints a fascinating picture of vampiric history. There are definitely shades of Underworld and Vampire: The Masquerade, but Vampire Therapist stays on the lighter side of things.

The motivations and backstory for your clients also manage to quite elegantly marry typical vampiric tropes with more realistic psychological conditions. Doctor Drayne struggles with addiction, only it’s to an experimental plasma he’s been making at his pharmaceutical company as part of a synthetic blood substitute.

The Contessa d’Este, meanwhile, is a literal Renaissance woman who struggles with depression and anger at the world, because after funding the great arts in her heyday, seeing Space Wars: Rise of Spacewalker (no prize for what that’s parodying) made her think all her efforts to enlighten humanity were for nought.

About the only issue I had is that Vampire Therapist, for what I played at least, seems to sidestep some more pressing issues and leaves a lot of questions unanswered about character motivations. Drayne, for example, is mostly concerned that his addiction to the experimental plasma is affecting his workaholic lifestyle, but the fact he has no memory of what happens when he consumes the substance isn’t given any attention at all.

Admittedly, it’d be a bit awkward to delve into Andromachos’ backstory as the Terror of the Mediterranean, and it might well be something brought up in latter parts of the story. Still, it can feel as though there are some elements that should be explored further, especially as we do get a lot of detail about Sam Walls’ path to redemption even in the first part of Vampire Therapist. What’s been biting at you this evening? Vampire Therapist doesn’t have much in the way of issues, as I see it. It’s a solid visual novel with an original premise, some intriguing puzzle gameplay and a genuinely fascinating, intriguing approach to explaining concepts of mental health and therapy that I found quite informative.

The only problem is that it does presume a lot about the player and how they want Sam Walls to act. Especially considering one early section has Walls nibbling (consensually) on some goth necks in a very sensually implied manner. It’s not as though you’re not warned that it delves into mature topics, but if you’re like me and couldn’t stand the romance in something like Baldur’s Gate 3 it can still feel a bit awkward.

But those are small potatoes, and my earlier mention of stuff I think the story could have done better is only evidence of how much I was paying attention to, and absorbing, the world and its characters, which I think is the highest praise you can give anything narrative-based. It’s lightweight, narrow in scale and perfect for relaxing while offering something of a challenge as you learn more about your clients and try to help them ground themselves and, if not resolve, then at least manage their problems. Let’s put a stake in this What I really liked about Vampire Therapist was how it connected the issues faced by immortals to real-world mental problems without seeming hokey. It’s not just that the Contessa is feeling depressed; it’s that seeing the world go from the Mona Lisa to Rise of Skywalker has seemingly given her concrete evidence that her years of patronage to the fine arts were worthless.

This makes Vampire Therapist feel worthwhile for fans of vampire fiction, as well as those just looking for a fascinating story. Add to that the intriguing explanations of therapy terms, psychological distortions and explorations of the (in)human condition, and ironically it feels as though a game about vampires helps to demystify the idea.

If you’re looking for a solid visual novel with a bit of meat on its bones, and some great art and audio that elevates what rather bashfully calls itself a low-budget indie, then Vampire Therapist is a must-play!

BYD in talks to buy Ineos’ French factory as Grenadier moves to US

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The French factory Mercedes built for the tiny Smart city car, and that Jim Ratcliffe turned into the home of his Land Rover Defender tribute, could soon be building BYDs.

BYD confirmed today that it has entered exclusive negotiations to acquire Ineos Automotive’s plant in Hambach, France. If it closes, it would be the Chinese giant’s second passenger car factory in the EU after Hungary, and the first time a Chinese automaker buys an existing car plant in Europe.

The other half of the deal is Ineos packing up. Alongside the announcement, Ineos Automotive said it plans to move Grenadier and Quartermaster production to the US, which it says accounts for about 70% of its sales. It has shortlisted US sites, hasn’t named any, and is aiming to start production there in 2028.

The math is simple. Every Grenadier shipped from France to the US gets hit with a 15% tariff, and the Quartermaster pickup falls under the 25% tariff on imported pickups. When 7 out of 10 of your customers are American, building your trucks in Lorraine stops making sense.

Ineos says it will keep building both models at Hambach “for the medium term” if the sale goes through, so the two companies would share the site for a while.

Neither side disclosed a price or a closing date, and the sale still has to go through consultation with employee representatives. BYD will soon find out that France is nothing like China when it comes to employee representation. There’s also no word yet on which BYD models would come out of Hambach or when. Same for the plant’s roughly 1,000 employees: BYD made no commitment on how many it would keep.

BYD executive vice president Stella Li said the company wants to give European consumers “vehicles built in France” that use BYD’s technology.

Hambach opened in 1997 as “Smartville,” Mercedes’ factory for the Smart Fortwo, and it later built the all-electric Smart EQ Fortwo. Ineos bought it from Mercedes at the end of 2020 after dropping its plan to build a new factory in Wales, and Grenadier production started in 2022. Ineos says it has put nearly €540 million into the site since 2019.

Capacity is about 30,000 Grenadiers a year. That’s not much by BYD’s standards. BYD sold 3,561,615 passenger vehicles globally in the first 10 months of the year, which means 30,000 units is less than three days of its output.

There’s also a bit of irony in who’s buying. Ineos unveiled its first EV, the Fusilier, in early 2024 while romanticizing gas cars at the same event. A few months later, it shelved the electric model indefinitely, blaming “reluctant consumer uptake” of EVs. Now it’s selling its only factory to the world’s largest EV maker.

This isn’t a surprise. Back in May, Stella Li said BYD was in talks with Stellantis and “other companies too” to take over underused plants in Europe, and that BYD would rather own and run a plant than form a joint venture. Hambach fits that description exactly.

BYD’s first European car plant, in Szeged, Hungary, started trial production earlier this year and is expected to reach mass production in early 2027, with the Dolphin Surf and Atto 2 first in line. BYD eventually wants more than 150,000 vehicles a year out of it. Its separate factory project in Turkey has been put on pause.

The motivation is regulatory. China-built BYD EVs face a 17% countervailing duty in the EU on top of the standard 10% car tariff, imported Chinese hybrids are now subject to quotas, and the EU’s proposed “Made in Europe” rules would tie EV subsidies and public contracts to how much of a vehicle is built locally. A French plant solves a lot of those problems at once.

And BYD isn’t alone. Leapmotor started building the B10 at Stellantis’ Zaragoza plant in Spain in October, Dongfeng plans to build cars at a Stellantis factory in France, Geely is reportedly lining up a Ford plant in Spain, and Chery already assembles vehicles in Barcelona.

Chery is the one BYD should watch. Through October, BYD registered 320,445 vehicles in Europe, up 133%, according to Automotive News. Chery Group was just ahead with 320,631, up 235%. A gap of 186 cars.

Europe matters more than ever for BYD because home isn’t going well. BYD brand sales in China fell 40% to 1,544,699 vehicles over the first 10 months, and its global passenger vehicle sales are down 2.7%.

This is a smart, cheap move for BYD. Building Szeged took years, including land disputes with private owners. Hambach comes with a trained workforce, a paint shop, a supplier base on the German border, and a seller that’s motivated to leave. You don’t get a much easier entry into France, but the easiest in France is still probably more difficult than anything BYD faced in China when it comes to deploying new production capacity.

The 30,000-unit capacity is small, but BYD isn’t buying it for what it builds today. It’s buying a “Made in France” label ahead of local content rules.

We also can’t ignore the symmetry here. Ineos is leaving Europe to build in the US because of tariffs. BYD is buying into Europe because of tariffs. That’s protectionism moving factories. Europe keeps the jobs, and the workers who used to build Smart EVs might get to build EVs again.

And yes, it’s funny that BYD is moving into the home of the Grenadier, a vehicle that exists because Ratcliffe loved the old Defender, days after a court in The Hague barred BYD’s Ti7 from the EU for infringing Land Rover’s Defender design.

The real question is what BYD puts on the line in Hambach. If it’s battery-electric cars, great. If it’s plug-in hybrids built to get around quotas, Europe is trading one gas-burning 4×4 factory for another.

If you’re buying a BYD or any other EV, pairing it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. It’s a free service with hundreds of pre-vetted installers competing for your business, so you save 20 to 30% compared to going it alone. No sales calls until you pick an installer. Trump Account contributions from friends and family pass $1 billion mark, Treasury says

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Families and friends have collectively contributed more than $1 billion to Trump Accounts since the July 4 launch, bringing total deposits to more than $9 billion, the U.S. Department of the Treasury said Tuesday in an announcement exclusively provided to CNBC.

The $1 billion in Trump Account deposits come from more than 3 million individuals, according to the agency. That total does not include the government's seed money or philanthropic gifts from donors such as Michael and Susan Dell.

"The American people are investing in Trump Accounts with a generosity that represents the finest instincts of our national character," U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in a written statement.

"Trump Accounts are a transformative government program for American children. It is the largest government start-up in American history," Internal Revenue Service CEO Frank Bisignano said in the statement.

Also known as 530A accounts, Trump Accounts are open to any U.S. child under 18 with a Social Security number.

Parents, guardians, grandparents and others can collectively contribute up to $5,000 per child per year in after-tax dollars up until the year before the beneficiary turns 18. The annual contribution limit is indexed for inflation after 2027.

Children born from 2025 through 2028 may receive a one-time $1,000 deposit from the Treasury. Other funds may also be available, depending on certain criteria.

Most recently, Snowflake chairman Frank Slootman and his wife, Brenda, announced they would give $1,000 to every child in Montana born in 2023 and 2024.

The Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump Accounts to hold stock donations, a move meant to pave the way for more philanthropic gifts, according to Bisignano.

These rules make it more attractive for donors, according to Ben Henry-Moreland, a certified financial planner with advisor platform Kitces.com.

Wealthy founders and shareholders can avoid the capital gains taxes that would otherwise be triggered by selling appreciated stock and donating the cash proceeds. However, it also means some children's wealth accumulation could be tied to the performance of individual companies.

The Trump Accounts milestone comes as more billionaires and companies pledge support to the investment account for kids.

In an exclusive interview with CNBC's "Squawk on the Street," Bessent on Nov. 20 announced an Anthropic co-founder would make a "large gift" to Trump Accounts over the next few weeks.

A source briefed on the announcement confirmed that the Anthropic co-founder Bessent referred to is Tom Brown and that it is part of his broader philanthropic plan.

"This is the perfect vehicle to donate to the children of America, and they will receive 100% of the proceeds," Bessent said.

Elon Musk wrote in an X post that he plans to give every kid in America a share of SpaceX sometime in 2027, which experts say may come via Trump Accounts.

In a separate funding announcement, SpaceX President Gwynne Shotwell said she would donate around 2 million shares of her and her husband's SpaceX stock to the accounts.

On Monday, Altimeter Capital CEO Brad Gerstner, founder of Invest America and an architect of Trump Accounts, told CNBC's "Halftime Report" that more large-scale donations may be in the pipeline.

"I want everybody to own shares of Nvidia, in Anthropic, in OpenAI," he said. "We created 70 million stock accounts — free stock accounts — for every single citizen under the age of 18 to do just that, and we have huge announcements coming."

Nick Carter Loses Bid to Dismiss Sexual Battery Claim From Melissa Schuman

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A Los Angeles County judge has rejected Backstreet Boys singer Nick Carter’s bid to dismiss a sexual battery claim brought by singer and actress Melissa Schuman.

In a new ruling obtained by Rolling Stone, the judge said the claim could proceed under a California law that took effect Jan. 1, opening a two-year revival window for otherwise time-barred claims of adult sexual abuse against individual perpetrators. Unlike the law in effect when Schuman first sued Carter, the new statute allows lawsuits against individuals without requiring plaintiffs to establish a cover-up or name a business as a defendant.

“The individual claim against defendant Carter for sexual battery is proper irrespective of the existence of a cover-up. Defendants cannot prevail on summary adjudication merely by attacking the cover-up allegations,” Los Angeles County Superior Court Judge Lisa K. Sepe-Wiesenfeld wrote in her ruling issued Monday. “There is a conflict in the evidence presented as to whether a sexual assault of plaintiff occurred. Accordingly, a triable issue of material fact is present as to the first cause of action [for sexual battery].”

Rolling Stone reached out to lawyers on both sides for comment but did not immediately hear back on Tuesday.

Carter had sought to dismiss Schuman’s sexual battery claim by challenging the purported cover-up alleged in her lawsuit. He argued in filings that her “entire complaint would be time-barred except for her cover-up claim” and that there was “no viable sexual battery claim against Carter individually” without the claim of an “underlying cover-up.”

In a declaration, Carter said, “At no point in time have I ever offered, solicited and/or secured a non-disclosure agreement, a confidentiality agreement, a payoff, a settlement and/or otherwise participated in silencing an alleged victim of sexual assault.”

At a Nov. 6 hearing, Carter’s lawyer, Dale Hayes Jr., argued that Schuman’s “only evidence” of a cover-up came from Shannon Ruth, who alleges Carter sexually assaulted her on tour bus outside the Tacoma Dome in Washington after a show on February 25, 2001. Ruth claims Carter told her the incident was their “special secret” and that she would go to jail if she reported it to police. Hayes argued to the judge that Ruth’s testimony was “heavily contradicted” by 13 other witnesses who testified that Carter had no opportunity to encounter Ruth the night of the alleged assault. Carter maintains he never met Ruth. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

After taking two weeks to consider the arguments, Judge Sepe-Wiesenfeld ruled in Schuman’s favor, allowing the sexual battery claim to proceed. A trial is set for May 10, 2027.

Schuman first sued Carter in April 2023, alleging he raped her at his Santa Monica apartment in 2003, when she was 18, and he was 23. The former member of the pop group Dream alleges Carter gave her a sedative-laced cocktail and performed oral sex on her over her protests before raping her. The two had been cast as co-stars in the 2004 made-for-TV horror film The Hollow.

Carter, 46, previously tried to dismiss the lawsuit, arguing that the dispute belonged in Nevada, where he was already suing Schuman and two other women who accused him of rape, alleging they conspired to defame and extort him. The judge rejected the request. In July 2024, he filed a $2.5 million cross-complaint against Schuman in California, maintaining that the two had consensual sex. Carter faces separate sexual assault claims in Nevada from Ruth and two other women. Ruth alleges Carter sexually assaulted her on a tour bus in 2001. Ashley Repp alleges Carter raped her on a yacht in 2003, when she was 15. Laura Penly sued Carter in April 2025, alleging he sexually assaulted her twice in 2005, when she was 19.

Carter has denied all four women’s allegations and has not been criminally charged in connection with their claims. His lawyers previously told Rolling Stone the women were part of a “gang of conspirators” seeking to capitalize on a resurgence in his career.
